# Contents

Contents

The Parliament has adopted this Act of the Czech Republic:

GENERAL PART

SCOPE OF CRIMINAL LAWS

No crime without law

Prohibition of retroactivity

An act is punishable only if its punishability was established by law before it was committed.

Temporal scope

Punishability of an act and the time of its commission

(1) The punishability of an act shall be assessed according to the law in force at the time the act was committed; it shall be assessed according to a later law only if that is more favourable for the offender.

(2) If the law changes during the commission of an act, the law in force at the completion of the conduct by which the act is committed shall apply.

(3) In the event of subsequent changes to the law that is in force at the completion of the conduct by which the act is committed, the most lenient law shall apply.

(4) An act is committed at the time when the perpetrator or participant acted or, in the case of omission, was obliged to act. It is not decisive when the consequence occurs or when it was to occur.

Application of the law in force at the time of decision

(1) Only such type of punishment may be imposed on the offender as is permitted by the law in force at the time the criminal offence is being decided.

(2) A protective measure shall always be decided according to the law in force at the time the protective measure is being decided. Territorial scope

Principle of territoriality

(1) The punishability of an act committed on the territory of the Czech Republic shall be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic.

(2) A criminal offence is deemed to have been committed on the territory of the Czech Republic,

a) if the perpetrator committed the conduct wholly or partially there, even if the violation or endangerment of an interest protected by criminal law occurred or was to occur wholly or partially abroad, or

b) if the perpetrator violated or endangered an interest protected by criminal law there or if such consequence was to occur there at least partially, even if the conduct was committed abroad.

(3) Participation is committed on the territory of the Czech Republic,

a) if the act of the perpetrator is committed there, where the place of commission of such act is assessed analogously according to paragraph 2, or

b) if a participant in an act committed abroad acted there partially.

(4) If a participant acted on the territory of the Czech Republic, the law of the Czech Republic shall apply to the participation regardless of whether the act of the perpetrator is punishable abroad.

Principle of registration

The punishability of an act committed outside the territory of the Czech Republic on board a ship or other vessel, or an aircraft or other aerial means of transport registered in the Czech Republic shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic. The place of commission of such act shall be assessed analogously according to § 4 paragraphs 2 and 3.

Principle of personality

The punishability of an act committed abroad by a citizen of the Czech Republic or a stateless person who has permanent residence on its territory shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic.

Principle of protection and principle of universality

(1) The punishability of torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment (§ 149), counterfeiting and alteration of money (§ 233), uttering of counterfeit and altered money (§ 235), production and possession of counterfeiting tools and equipment intended for unauthorised obtaining of a means of payment (§ 236), unauthorised production of money (§ 237), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), threatening with a terrorist criminal offence (§ 312f), sabotage (§ 314), espionage (§ 316), violence against a public authority (§ 323), violence against an official (§ 325), counterfeiting and alteration of public documents (§ 348), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), preparation of aggressive war (§ 406), use of prohibited combat means and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of population (§ 413), pillaging in the area of military operations (§ 414), abuse of internationally recognised and state signs (§ 415), abuse of flag and truce (§ 416) and injury to a member of parliament (§ 417) shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic even if such criminal offence was committed abroad by a foreign national or a stateless person who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic.

(2) The punishability of an act committed abroad against a citizen of the Czech Republic or against a stateless person who has permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic shall be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if the act is punishable at the place of its commission or if the place of commission of the act is not subject to any criminal jurisdiction.

Subsidiary principle of universality

(1) The punishability of an act committed abroad by a foreign national or a stateless person who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if

a) the act is also punishable under the law in force in the territory where it was committed,

b) the perpetrator was apprehended on the territory of the Czech Republic, extradition or surrender proceedings took place and the perpetrator was not extradited or surrendered for criminal prosecution or execution of punishment to a foreign state or other authorised entity, and

c) the foreign state or other authorised entity that requested the extradition or surrender of the perpetrator for criminal prosecution or execution of punishment has requested that criminal prosecution of the perpetrator be conducted in the Czech Republic.

(2) The punishability of an act committed abroad by a foreign national or a stateless person who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if the act was committed for the benefit of a legal person that has its seat or organisational unit on the territory of the Czech Republic.

(3) However, no punishment more severe than that provided for by the law of the state on whose territory the criminal offence was committed may be imposed on the perpetrator. Scope established by international treaty

(1) The punishability of an act shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if so provided by an international treaty that forms part of the legal order (hereinafter "international treaty").

(2) The provisions of § 4 to 8 shall not apply if the international treaty does not permit it.

## § 10 repealed

Effects of a Foreign State's Judgment

(1) A criminal judgment of a foreign state cannot be executed in the territory of the Czech Republic nor have other effects here, unless the law or an international treaty provides otherwise. (2) A final conviction by a court of another member state of the European Union in criminal proceedings shall be treated for the purposes of criminal proceedings as a conviction by a court of the Czech Republic, if it was issued for an act punishable also under the law of the Czech Republic. CRIMINAL LIABILITY

Foundations of Criminal Liability

Principle of Legality and Principle of Subsidiarity of Criminal Repression

(1) Only criminal law defines criminal offenses and establishes criminal sanctions that may be imposed for their commission. (2) Criminal liability of the perpetrator and the criminal law consequences connected therewith may be applied only in socially harmful cases where the application of liability under another legal regulation does not suffice. Criminal Offense

(1) A criminal offense is an unlawful act which is designated as criminal by criminal law and which exhibits the characteristics specified in such law. (2) Intent is required for criminal liability for a criminal offense, unless criminal law expressly provides that negligence suffices. Misdemeanors and Felonies

(1) Criminal offenses are divided into misdemeanors and felonies. (2) Misdemeanors are all negligent criminal offenses and those intentional criminal offenses for which criminal law prescribes imprisonment with an upper limit of the penalty range up to five years. (3) Felonies are all criminal offenses which are not misdemeanors under criminal law; particularly serious felonies are those intentional criminal offenses for which criminal law prescribes imprisonment with an upper limit of the penalty range of at least ten years. Culpability

Intent

(1) A criminal offense is committed intentionally if the perpetrator

a) intended to violate or endanger an interest protected by such law in the manner specified in criminal law, or

b) knew that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, and in the event that he would cause it, he accepted it. (2) Acceptance also means the perpetrator's reconciliation with the fact that he may violate or endanger an interest protected by such law in the manner specified in criminal law. Negligence

(1) A criminal offense is committed negligently if the perpetrator

a) knew that he could violate or endanger an interest protected by such law in the manner specified in criminal law, but relied, without reasonable grounds, that he would not cause such violation or endangerment, or

b) did not know that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, although he should have and could have known this given the circumstances and his personal situation. (2) A criminal offense is committed with gross negligence if the perpetrator's approach to the requirement of due care evidences the perpetrator's obvious disregard for interests protected by criminal law. Culpability as to Aggravating Circumstances

A circumstance which conditions the application of a higher penalty range shall be taken into account,

a) if it is a more serious consequence, even if the perpetrator was negligent as to it, except where criminal law requires intent even in such case, or

b) if it is another fact, even if the perpetrator did not know about it, although he should have and could have known about it given the circumstances and his personal situation, except where criminal law requires that the perpetrator knew about it. Mistake of Fact

(1) A person who, when committing an act, does not know and does not consider as possible a factual circumstance which is an element of a criminal offense does not act intentionally; this is without prejudice to liability for a criminal offense committed negligently. (2) A person who, when committing an act, mistakenly assumes factual circumstances which would constitute the elements of a less serious intentional criminal offense shall be punished only for this less serious criminal offense, unless it is a criminal offense committed negligently. (3) A person who, when committing an act, mistakenly assumes factual circumstances which would constitute the elements of a more serious intentional criminal offense shall be punished for an attempt of this more serious criminal offense. (4) A person who, when committing an act, mistakenly assumes a factual circumstance which excludes its unlawfulness does not act intentionally; this is without prejudice to liability for a criminal offense committed negligently. Mistake of Law

(1) A person who, when committing a criminal offense, does not know that his act is unlawful does not act culpably if he could not have avoided the mistake. (2) The mistake could have been avoided if the duty to become acquainted with the relevant legal regulation arose for the perpetrator from law or other legal regulation, official decision or contract, from his employment, profession, position or function, or if the perpetrator could have recognized the unlawfulness of the act without obvious difficulties. Preparation for and Attempt of a Criminal Offense

Preparation

(1) Conduct consisting of intentionally creating conditions for committing a particularly serious felony (§ 14(3)), in particular organizing it, procuring or adapting means or instruments for its commission, conspiring, assembling, inciting or aiding such felony, is preparation only if criminal law expressly provides so for the respective criminal offense and if there was no attempt or completion of the particularly serious felony. (2) Preparation is punishable according to the penalty range prescribed for the particularly serious felony toward which it was directed, unless criminal law provides otherwise. (3) Criminal liability for preparation for a particularly serious felony expires if the perpetrator voluntarily abandoned further conduct directed at committing the particularly serious felony and

a) removed the danger which arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken preparation, or

b) made a report of the preparation for the particularly serious felony at a time when the danger which arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken preparation could still be removed; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or a police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. (4) If more persons participate in the act, the expiry of criminal liability for preparation does not apply to a perpetrator who so acted if the act is completed by other perpetrators independently of his earlier contribution to the act or despite his timely report. (5) The provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 are without prejudice to the criminal liability of the perpetrator for another completed criminal offense which he already committed by the conduct specified in paragraph 1. Attempt

(1) Conduct which directly aims at completing a criminal offense and which the perpetrator performed with the intent to commit the criminal offense is an attempt of a criminal offense if the criminal offense was not completed. (2) An attempt of a criminal offense is punishable according to the penalty range prescribed for the completed criminal offense. (3) Criminal liability for an attempt of a criminal offense expires if the perpetrator voluntarily abandoned further conduct directed at completing the criminal offense and

a) removed the danger which arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken attempt of the criminal offense, or

b) made a report of the attempt of the criminal offense at a time when the danger which arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken attempt of the criminal offense could still be removed; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or a police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. (4) If more persons participate in the act, the expiry of criminal liability for the attempt does not apply to a perpetrator who so acted if the act is completed by other perpetrators independently of his earlier contribution to the act or despite his timely report. (5) The provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 are without prejudice to the criminal liability of the perpetrator for another completed criminal offense which he already committed by the conduct specified in paragraph 1. Perpetrator, Co-Perpetrator and Participant in a Criminal Offense

Perpetrator

(1) The perpetrator of a criminal offense is a person who by his conduct fulfilled the elements of the factual basis of a criminal offense or its attempt or preparation, if punishable. (2) The perpetrator of a criminal offense is also a person who used another person to carry out the act, where such person is not criminally liable due to lack of age, insanity, mistake, or because he acted in necessary defense, extreme necessity or other circumstances excluding unlawfulness, or did not act or did not act culpably. The perpetrator of a criminal offense is also a person who used a person who did not act with a special intent or motive required by law to carry out the act; in these cases the criminal liability of such person for another criminal offense which he committed by this conduct is not excluded. Co-Perpetrator

If a criminal offense was committed by intentional joint conduct of two or more persons, each of them is liable as if he committed the criminal offense alone (co-perpetrators). Participant

(1) A participant in a completed criminal offense or its attempt is a person who intentionally

a) devised or directed the commission of the criminal offense (organizer),

b) induced another's decision to commit the criminal offense (inciter), or

c) enabled or facilitated another's commission of the criminal offense, in particular by procuring means, removing obstacles, luring the injured party to the scene of the act, keeping watch during the act, giving advice, strengthening the resolution or promising to assist after the criminal offense (aider). (2) The provisions on criminal liability and punishability of the perpetrator shall apply to the criminal liability and punishability of the participant, unless criminal law provides otherwise. (3) Criminal liability of the participant expires if he voluntarily abandoned further participation in the criminal offense and

a) removed the danger which arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken participation, or

b) made a report of the participation in the criminal offense at a time when the danger which arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken participation could still be removed; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or a police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. (4) If more persons participate in the act, the expiry of criminal liability does not apply to a participant who so acted if the act is committed by other perpetrators independently of his earlier contribution to the act or despite his timely report. (5) The provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 do not apply to the criminal liability of the participant for another criminal offense which he already committed by the conduct specified in paragraph 1. Age

A person who at the time of committing the act had not reached the age of fifteen is not criminally liable. Insanity

A person who, due to a mental disorder at the time of committing the act, could not recognize its unlawfulness or control his conduct is not criminally liable for this act. Diminished Capacity

A person who, due to a mental disorder at the time of committing the act, had substantially reduced capacity to recognize its unlawfulness or control his conduct, has diminished capacity. CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING UNLAWFULNESS OF AN ACT

Extreme Necessity

(1) An otherwise criminal act by which a person averts danger directly threatening an interest protected by criminal law is not a criminal offense. (2) Extreme necessity does not apply if under the given circumstances it was possible to avert this danger otherwise or if the consequence caused is obviously equally serious or even more serious than the one that was threatened, or if the person to whom the danger was threatening was obliged to bear it. Necessary Defense

(1) An otherwise criminal act by which a person averts a directly threatening or ongoing attack on an interest protected by criminal law is not a criminal offense. (2) Necessary defense does not apply if the defense was completely obviously disproportionate to the manner of attack. Consent of the Injured Party

(1) A person does not commit a criminal offense who acts on the basis of the consent of a person whose interests, about which this person may decide without limitation lawfully, are affected by the act. (2) Consent under paragraph 1 must be given in advance or simultaneously with the conduct of the person committing the otherwise criminal act, voluntarily, specifically, seriously and intelligibly; if such consent is given only after commission of the act, the perpetrator is not punishable if he could reasonably assume that the person specified in paragraph 1 would otherwise have granted this consent given the circumstances of the case and his situation. (3) With the exception of cases of consent to medical procedures which at the time of the act are in accordance with the legal order and the knowledge of medical science and practice, consent under paragraph 1 cannot be considered to be consent to bodily harm or killing. Permissible Risk

(1) A person does not commit a criminal offense who, in accordance with the achieved state of knowledge and information which he had at the time of his decision about further procedure, carries out socially beneficial activity within the scope of his employment, profession, position or function, by which he endangers or violates an interest protected by criminal law, if the socially beneficial result cannot be achieved otherwise. (2) Permissible risk does not apply if such activity endangers the life or health of a person without his consent given in accordance with another legal regulation, or if the result toward which it is directed obviously does not correspond to the degree of risk, or if the performance of this activity obviously contradicts the requirements of another legal regulation, public interest, principles of humanity or is contrary to good morals. Lawful Use of a Weapon

A person does not commit a criminal offense who uses a weapon within the limits prescribed by another legal regulation. EXPIRY OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY

Expiry of Criminal Liability by Effective Remorse

Effective Remorse

Criminal liability for the criminal offenses of failure to provide assistance (§ 150), failure to provide assistance by driver of a vehicle (§ 151), spreading a contagious human disease (§ 152), spreading a contagious human disease by negligence (§ 153), endangering health with defective foodstuffs and other items (§ 156), endangering health with defective foodstuffs and other items by negligence (§ 157), entrusting a child to another's power (§ 169), abduction (§ 172), hostage taking (§ 174), abandoning a child or entrusted person (§ 195), breach of duty in administering another's property (§ 220), breach of duty in administering another's property by negligence (§ 221), breach of duty in insolvency proceedings (§ 225), breach of duty to make a truthful declaration of property (§ 227), damage to another's property (§ 228), reduction of tax, fee and similar mandatory payment (§ 240), failure to fulfill reporting obligation in tax proceedings (§ 243), endangerment of foreign exchange management (§ 247), breach of regulations on circulation of goods in dealings with foreign countries (§ 261), breach of regulations on control of export of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 262), breach of duties in export of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 263), conducting foreign trade in military material without authorization or license (§ 265), breach of duty in connection with issuance of authorization and license for foreign trade in military material (§ 266), general endangerment (§ 272), general endangerment by negligence (§ 273), breach of duty in imminent distress (§ 275), damage and endangerment of operation of a generally beneficial facility (§ 276), damage and endangerment of operation of a generally beneficial facility by negligence (§ 277), damage to a geodetic point (§ 278), unlawful armament (§ 279), damage and endangerment of the environment (§ 293), damage and endangerment of the environment by negligence (§ 294), damage to forest (§ 295), unauthorized handling of waste (§ 298), unauthorized handling of protected free-living animals and wild-growing plants (§ 299), unauthorized handling of protected free-living animals and wild-growing plants by negligence (§ 300), spreading a contagious disease of animals (§ 306), spreading a contagious disease and pest of cultivated plants (§ 307), high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), sabotage (§ 314), abuse of representation of the state and international organization (§ 315), espionage (§ 316), endangerment of classified information (§ 317), endangerment of classified information by negligence (§ 318), participation in a non-state armed group aimed at operating in armed conflict (§ 321a), revolt of prisoners (§ 344), false accusation (§ 345), false testimony and false expert opinion (§ 346), false interpretation (§ 347), obstruction of justice (§ 347a), spreading an alarming message (§ 357), failure to prevent a criminal offense (§ 367), failure to report a criminal offense (§ 368) expires if the perpetrator voluntarily

a) prevented or remedied the harmful consequence of the criminal offense, or

b) made a report of the criminal offense at a time when the harmful consequence of the criminal offense could still be prevented; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or a police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. Limitation of Criminal Liability

Limitation Period

(1) Criminal liability for a criminal offense expires upon the lapse of the limitation period, which is

a) thirty years, if it is a criminal offense for which criminal law permits imposition of an exceptional punishment, and a criminal offense committed in the preparation or approval of a privatization project under another legal regulation,

b) fifteen years, if the upper limit of the penalty range of imprisonment is at least ten years,

c) ten years, if the upper limit of the penalty range of imprisonment is at least five years,

d) five years, if the upper limit of the penalty range of imprisonment is at least three years,

e) three years for other criminal offenses. (2) The limitation period begins to run for criminal offenses where the effect is an element or where the effect is an element of a qualified factual basis, from the moment when such effect occurred; for other criminal offenses the limitation period begins to run from the completion of the conduct. For a participant the limitation period begins to run from the completion of the act by the principal perpetrator. (3) The limitation period does not include

a) time during which it was not possible to bring the perpetrator before a court due to a statutory obstacle,

b) time during which criminal prosecution was suspended,

c) time during which the victim of the criminal offense of serious bodily harm consisting of mutilation of sexual organs or sterilization (§ 145), unlawful termination of pregnancy without consent of the pregnant woman (§ 159), trafficking in human beings (§ 168), abduction committed for the purpose of forcing another to marriage (§ 172), extortion (§ 175) or oppression (§ 177) committed for the purpose of forcing another to marriage or to tolerate an intervention aimed at causing serious bodily harm consisting of mutilation of sexual organs or of a criminal offense specified in chapter three of the special part of this law on criminal offenses against human dignity in the sexual sphere or the criminal offense of inducement to sexual intercourse (§ 202) was under eighteen years of age,

d) probation period of conditional suspension of criminal prosecution or conditional postponement of filing a proposal for punishment,

e) time during which it was not possible to criminally prosecute the perpetrator in the Czech Republic, if it is an act whose criminality is assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic on the basis of § 8(1),

f) time from issuance of an arrest warrant until its revocation or until its validity lapses for another reason,

g) time during which certain acts of criminal proceedings were temporarily omitted according to the Act on International Judicial Cooperation in Criminal Matters,

h) time during which criminal prosecution was temporarily suspended. (4) The limitation period is interrupted by

a) initiation of criminal prosecution for the criminal offense whose limitation is concerned, as well as by the subsequent taking into custody, issuance of an arrest warrant, filing of a request for securing extradition of a person from a foreign state, issuance of a European arrest warrant, filing of an indictment, proposal for approval of an agreement on guilt and punishment, proposal for punishment, pronouncement of a conviction judgment for this criminal offense or delivery of a penal order for such criminal offense to the accused, or

b) if the perpetrator committed a new criminal offense during the limitation period for which criminal law prescribes a punishment equal or more severe. (5) Upon interruption of the limitation period, a new limitation period begins. Exclusion from Limitation

Criminal liability does not expire upon lapse of the limitation period

a) for criminal offenses specified in chapter thirteen of the special part of this law, with the exception of the criminal offenses of establishing, supporting and promoting a movement aimed at suppressing human rights and freedoms (§ 403), expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing human rights and freedoms (§ 404), denial, questioning, approval and justification of genocide (§ 405), even if acts which would now have the characteristics of these criminal offenses were committed in the past,

b) for the criminal offenses of high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311) and terror (§ 312), if they were committed under such circumstances that they constitute a war crime or crime against humanity under the rules of international law,

c) for other criminal offenses committed in the period from February 25, 1948 to December 29, 1989, for which the upper limit of the penalty range of imprisonment is at least ten years, if for reasons incompatible with the fundamental principles of the legal order of a democratic state there was no final conviction or acquittal, and which were committed by public officials or were committed in connection with persecution of an individual or group of persons for political, racial or religious reasons. CRIMINAL SANCTIONS

Types of Criminal Sanctions and General Principles for Their Imposition

Types of Criminal Sanctions

Criminal sanctions are punishments and protective measures. General Provision for Imposing Criminal Sanctions

(1) Criminal sanctions may only be imposed on the basis of criminal law. (2) Cruel and disproportionate criminal sanctions may not be imposed on the perpetrator. The execution of a criminal sanction must not degrade human dignity. Proportionality of Criminal Sanctions

(1) Criminal sanctions must be imposed with regard to the nature and seriousness of the committed criminal offense and the circumstances of the perpetrator. (2) Where imposition of a criminal sanction less affecting the perpetrator suffices, a criminal sanction more sensitive for the perpetrator must not be imposed. (3) When imposing criminal sanctions, regard shall also be had to the legally protected interests of persons injured by the criminal offense. Punishments

General Principles for Imposing Punishments

Determination of the Type and Measure of Punishment

(1) When determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall have regard to the nature and seriousness of the committed criminal offense, to the personal, family, property and other circumstances of the perpetrator and to his previous way of life and to the possibility of his reform; it shall further have regard to the perpetrator's conduct after the act, in particular to his effort to compensate the damage or remove other harmful consequences of the act; it shall also have regard to his attitude to the criminal offense in criminal proceedings, whether he negotiated an agreement on guilt and punishment, declared his guilt or designated decisive facts as undisputed, and if he was designated as a cooperating accused, also to how significantly he contributed to clarification of a felony committed by members of an organized group, in connection with an organized group or for the benefit of an organized criminal group. It shall also have regard to the effects and consequences which can be expected from the punishment for the future life of the perpetrator. (2) The nature and seriousness of the criminal offense are determined in particular by the importance of the protected interest which was affected by the act, the manner of execution of the act and its consequences, the circumstances under which the act was committed, the person of the perpetrator, the degree of his culpability and his motive, intent or objective. (3) When determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall have regard to mitigating and aggravating circumstances (§ 41 and 42), to the time which has elapsed since the commission of the criminal offense, to any change in the situation and to the length of the criminal proceedings if it lasted disproportionately long. When assessing the proportionality of the length of criminal proceedings, the court shall have regard to the complexity of the case, to the procedure of authorities active in criminal proceedings, to the significance of criminal proceedings for the perpetrator and to his conduct by which he contributed to delays in criminal proceedings. (4) If the court does not impose a cumulative punishment or a joint punishment for continuation in a criminal offense, when determining the type of punishment and its measure it shall also have regard to the type and measure of punishments which were imposed on the perpetrator for his other criminal activity and have not yet been executed, so that, given the nature and seriousness of the criminal offense and the person of the perpetrator, such punishment is not imposed which together with punishments not yet executed would lead to disproportionate punishment of the perpetrator. (5) A circumstance which is a statutory element of a criminal offense, including a circumstance which conditions the application of a higher penalty range, cannot be taken into account as a mitigating or aggravating circumstance. A circumstance justifying extraordinary reduction of imprisonment cannot be taken into account as a mitigating circumstance. (6) An aggravating circumstance shall be taken into account,

a) if it is a more serious consequence, even if the perpetrator was negligent as to it, except where criminal law requires intent even in such case,

b) if it is another fact, even if the perpetrator did not know about it, although he should have and could have known about it given the circumstances and his personal situation, except where criminal law requires that the perpetrator knew about it. (7) When determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall have regard

a) in the case of co-perpetrators also to the extent to which the conduct of each of them contributed to the commission of the criminal offense,

b) in the case of an organizer, inciter and aider also to the significance and nature of their participation in the commission of the criminal offense,

c) in the case of preparation for a particularly serious felony and of an attempt of a criminal offense also to the extent to which the perpetrator's conduct approached the completion of the criminal offense, as well as to the circumstances and reasons for which it was not completed. (8) If the perpetrator obtained or attempted to obtain property benefit by the criminal offense, the court shall have regard thereto when determining the type of punishment and its measure; if his property or personal circumstances do not preclude it, it shall impose on him, having regard to the amount of such property benefit, one of the punishments which will affect him in property (§ 66 to 72), either as an independent punishment or alongside another punishment. The court shall particularly consider imposition of a pecuniary punishment (§ 67 to 69). Imposing Punishment on a Perpetrator with Diminished Capacity

(1) If the perpetrator committed a criminal offense in a state of diminished capacity which he did not, even negligently, bring upon himself by the influence of an addictive substance, the court shall have regard to this circumstance when determining the type of punishment and its measure. (2) If the court is of the opinion that, given the health condition of the perpetrator specified in paragraph 1, it would be possible, while simultaneously imposing protective treatment (§ 99), to achieve the possibility of his reform even with a punishment of shorter duration, it shall reduce the imprisonment below the lower limit of the penalty range, whereby it is not bound by the limitation specified in § 58(4), and shall simultaneously impose protective treatment. Mitigating Circumstances

The court shall have regard as a mitigating circumstance in particular to the fact that the perpetrator

a) committed a criminal offense for the first time and under circumstances independent of him,

b) committed a criminal offense in strong agitation, out of compassion or due to lack of life experience,

c) committed a criminal offense under pressure of dependence or subordination,

d) committed a criminal offense under the influence of threat or coercion,

e) committed a criminal offense under the influence of distressing personal or family circumstances which he did not cause himself,

f) committed a criminal offense at an age close to the age of juveniles,

g) committed a criminal offense averting an attack or other danger, although the conditions of necessary defense or extreme necessity were not completely fulfilled, or exceeded the limits of permissible risk or limits of another circumstance excluding unlawfulness,

h) committed a criminal offense in a mistake of law which could have been avoided,

i) caused lesser damage or other lesser harmful consequence by the criminal offense,

j) endeavored to remove the harmful consequences of the criminal offense or voluntarily compensated the damage caused,

k) reported his criminal offense himself to the authorities,

l) confessed to the criminal offense,

m) assisted in clarifying his criminal activity or significantly contributed to clarification of a criminal offense committed by another,

n) contributed, in particular as a cooperating accused, to clarification of criminal activity committed by members of an organized group, in connection with an organized group or for the benefit of an organized criminal group,

o) sincerely regretted the criminal offense, or

p) led a proper life before committing the criminal offense. Aggravating Circumstances

The court shall have regard as an aggravating circumstance in particular to the fact that the perpetrator

a) committed a criminal offense with premeditation or after prior consideration,

b) committed a criminal offense out of greed, vengeance, national, racial, ethnic, religious, class or other similar hatred or out of another particularly reprehensible motive,

c) committed a criminal offense in a cruel or torturous manner, treacherously, with special cunning or in another similar manner,

d) committed a criminal offense taking advantage of someone's need, distress, defenselessness, dependence or subordination,

e) violated a special duty by the criminal offense,

f) abused his employment, position or function for committing the criminal offense,

g) committed a criminal offense against a person participating in rescue of life and health or in protection of property,

h) committed a criminal offense against a child, close person, pregnant, sick, disabled, elderly or helpless person and thereby endangered their life or health, caused them damage, injury to health or other harm or unjustly enriched himself at their expense,

i) induced another, in particular a child under fifteen years of age, a juvenile or a person of an age close to the age of juveniles, to an otherwise criminal act, misdemeanor or criminal offense,

j) committed a criminal offense during a crisis situation, natural disaster or other event seriously threatening life, public order or property, or in a territory in which evacuation is being or has been carried out,

k) caused higher damage or other greater harmful consequence by the criminal offense,

l) obtained higher benefit by the criminal offense,

m) committed a criminal offense on a larger scale, on more items or more persons, or committed the criminal offense or continued in it for a longer period,

n) committed more criminal offenses,

o) committed a criminal offense with another person,

p) committed a criminal offense as an organizer, as a member of an organized group or a member of an association, or

q) has already been convicted for a criminal offense; the court is entitled, according to the nature of the previous conviction, not to consider this circumstance as aggravating, in particular with regard to the importance of the protected interest which was affected by the act, the manner of execution of the act and its consequences, the circumstances under which the act was committed, the person of the perpetrator, the degree of his culpability, his motive and the time which has elapsed since the last conviction, and if it is a perpetrator of a criminal offense which was committed in a state induced by mental disorder, or a perpetrator who indulges in abuse of an addictive substance and committed a criminal offense under its influence or in connection with its abuse, also if he commenced treatment or took other necessary measures for its initiation. Aggregate and Cumulative Punishment

(1) If the court convicts a perpetrator for two or more criminal offenses, it shall impose on him an aggregate punishment according to the provision which relates to the most severely punishable criminal offense among them; if it is a multi-act concurrence of a greater number of criminal offenses, the court may impose on the perpetrator imprisonment within the penalty range whose upper limit is increased by one third; however, the upper limit of the penalty range of imprisonment must not, even after this increase, exceed twenty years and when imposing exceptional imprisonment over twenty up to thirty years must not exceed thirty years. Alongside the punishment admissible under such provision, another type of punishment may be imposed within the aggregate punishment if its imposition would be justified by any of the adjudicated criminal offenses. If the lower limits of the penalty ranges of imprisonment are different, the highest of them is the lower limit of the aggregate punishment. If criminal law prescribes only imprisonment for any of such criminal offenses, the aggregate punishment may only be imprisonment as an independent punishment. (2) The court shall impose cumulative punishment according to the principles specified in paragraph 1 when it convicts a perpetrator for a criminal offense which he committed before a conviction judgment for another his criminal offense was pronounced by the court of first instance. Together with imposition of cumulative punishment, the court shall annul the part of the judgment on the punishment imposed on the perpetrator by the earlier judgment, as well as all other decisions substantively connected to this part, insofar as they lost their basis due to the change which occurred by this annulment. The cumulative punishment must not be more lenient than the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment. Within the cumulative punishment, the court must pronounce the punishment of loss of honorary titles or decorations, punishment of loss of military rank, punishment of forfeiture of property or punishment of forfeiture of a thing if such punishment was already pronounced by the earlier judgment. (3) A conviction judgment under paragraph 2 also means a judgment by which punishment was conditionally waived with supervision under the conditions of § 48(1). Together with imposition of cumulative punishment, the court shall annul the part of the judgment on conditional waiver of punishment with supervision, as well as all other decisions substantively connected to this part, insofar as they lost their basis due to the change which occurred by the annulment. (4) The provisions on cumulative punishment shall not apply if the earlier conviction is of such nature that the perpetrator is treated as if he had not been convicted, or if the earlier conviction judgment was issued by a court of another member state of the European Union. Waiver of Imposition of Cumulative Punishment

The court shall waive the imposition of cumulative punishment according to § 43(2) if it is of the opinion that the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment is sufficient.Imposition of Cumulative Sentence for Continuing Criminal Offense

(1) If the court convicts the perpetrator for a partial attack in a continuing criminal offense (§ 116), for whose other attacks he was convicted by a judgment that has already become final, it shall annul in this earlier judgment the finding of guilt concerning the continuing criminal offense and criminal offenses committed in concurrence therewith, the entire pronouncement on punishment, as well as other pronouncements that have their basis in the stated finding of guilt, and shall again, while being bound by the factual findings in the annulled judgment, decide on guilt for the continuing criminal offense, including the new partial attack, or criminal offenses committed in concurrence therewith, on a cumulative sentence for the continuing criminal offense, which may not be more lenient than the sentence imposed by the earlier judgment, and possibly also on consequent pronouncements that have their basis in the finding of guilt. Within the framework of the cumulative sentence for continuing a criminal offense, the court must pronounce a sentence of loss of honorary titles or decorations, loss of military rank, forfeiture of property or forfeiture of a thing, if such a sentence was already pronounced by the earlier judgment.

(2) The provisions of § 43 and 44 shall be applied accordingly in cases where a sentence is being imposed simultaneously for several criminal offenses.

(3) The provisions on cumulative sentence for continuing a criminal offense shall also apply if the earlier conviction is of such a nature that the perpetrator is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

(4) The provisions on cumulative sentence for continuing a criminal offense shall not apply if the earlier conviction judgment was issued by a court of another Member State of the European Union.

Release from Punishment

General Provision

(1) Release from punishment may be granted for a perpetrator who committed a misdemeanor, regrets its commission and shows effective effort at reform, if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanor committed and to the perpetrator's life to date, it can be reasonably expected that mere adjudication of the matter will suffice for his reform and for the protection of society.

(2) The court shall release from punishment a perpetrator designated as a cooperating accused if the conditions set out in § 178a paragraphs 1 and 2 of the Code of Criminal Procedure are met and if the cooperating accused provided both in the preparatory proceedings and in the proceedings before the court a complete and truthful testimony about facts that are capable of significantly contributing to the clarification of a felony committed by members of an organized group, in connection with an organized group or for the benefit of an organized criminal group; the provision of § 48 paragraph 1 is not affected thereby. Release from punishment of a perpetrator designated as a cooperating accused cannot be granted in this manner if the criminal offense committed by him is more serious than the felony to whose clarification he contributed, if he participated as an organizer or instigator in the commission of the felony to whose clarification he contributed, if he intentionally caused serious bodily harm or death, or if there are grounds for extraordinary increase of the sentence of imprisonment (§ 59).

(3) The court may also release from punishment if the perpetrator of preparation or attempt of a criminal offense did not recognize that the preparation or attempt, having regard to the nature or type of the object of the attack on which the act was to be committed, or having regard to the nature or type of the means by which the act was to be committed, could not lead to completion.

(4) If the court has released from punishment, the perpetrator is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

Release from Punishment with Concurrent Imposition of Protective Treatment or Preventive Detention

(1) The court may also release from punishment if the perpetrator committed a criminal offense in a state of diminished sanity or in a state induced by a mental disorder, and the court considers that protective treatment (§ 99), which it imposes at the same time, will ensure the perpetrator's reform and the protection of society better than punishment. This provision shall not apply if the perpetrator induced the state of diminished sanity or mental disorder, even through negligence, by the influence of an addictive substance.

(2) The court may also release from punishment if the perpetrator committed a felony in a state of diminished sanity or in a state induced by a mental disorder, and it cannot be expected that the imposed protective treatment, having regard to the nature of the mental disorder and the possibilities of influencing the perpetrator, would lead to sufficient protection of society, and the court considers that preventive detention (§ 100), which it imposes on the perpetrator at the same time, will ensure the protection of society better than punishment.

Conditional Release from Punishment with Supervision

(1) Under the conditions stated in § 46 paragraphs 1, 2 or 3, the court may conditionally release from punishment and establish supervision over the perpetrator if it considers it necessary to monitor the perpetrator's behavior for a specified period.

(2) In conditional release from punishment, the court shall establish a probation period of up to one year and at the same time impose supervision on the perpetrator (§ 49 to 51).

(3) The court may impose appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations on the perpetrator from whose punishment has been conditionally released, aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; it shall as a rule also impose on him to make reparation for damage or redress for non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offense according to his abilities, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offense.

(4) The court may impose as appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations in particular

a) to undergo training to obtain suitable work qualifications,

b) to undergo a suitable program of social training and re-education,

c) to undergo treatment for addiction to addictive substances, which is not protective treatment under this Act,

d) to undergo suitable programs of psychological counseling,

e) to refrain from visiting unsuitable environments, sports, cultural and other social events and contact with certain persons,

f) to refrain from unauthorized interference with the rights or legally protected interests of other persons,

g) to refrain from gambling, playing gaming machines and betting,

h) to refrain from consuming alcoholic beverages or other addictive substances,

i) to pay owed maintenance or other owed amount,

j) to publicly apologize personally to the injured party, or

k) to provide reasonable satisfaction to the injured party.

(5) In the case of a perpetrator of an age close to that of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilizing the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations referred to in paragraph 4, also some of the educational measures referred to in the Act on Juvenile Justice, with analogous application of the conditions established for juveniles.

(6) If the perpetrator from whose punishment has been conditionally released leads a proper life during the probation period and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may cancel the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them, or may cancel the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his behavior to an increased extent; the court may make this decision no earlier than after the expiry of six months of the imposed probation period. The established probation period remains unaffected by the decision according to the first sentence.

(7) If the perpetrator from whose punishment has been conditionally released led a proper life during the probation period and complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide on the imposition of punishment, possibly already during the probation period.

(8) If the court has not made a decision according to paragraph 7 within one year from the expiry of the probation period, without the perpetrator from whose punishment has been conditionally released being at fault, it shall be deemed that he has proved himself.

(9) If it has been declared that the perpetrator from whose punishment has been conditionally released has proved himself, or if it is deemed that he has proved himself, he is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

Supervision

Concept and Purpose of Supervision

(1) Supervision means regular personal contact of the perpetrator with an officer of the Probation and Mediation Service (hereinafter "probation officer"), cooperation in creating and implementing the probation supervision plan and control of compliance with the conditions imposed on the perpetrator by the court or arising from the law.

a) monitoring and control of the perpetrator's behavior, whereby the protection of society and reduction of the possibility of repeat criminal activity is ensured,

b) professional guidance and assistance to the perpetrator with the aim of ensuring that he leads a proper life in the future.

(3) Supervision over the perpetrator is carried out by a probation officer.

Obligations of the Perpetrator

A perpetrator on whom supervision has been imposed is obliged to

a) cooperate with the probation officer in the manner determined by the probation officer and fulfill the probation supervision plan,

b) report to the probation officer within the time limits determined by the probation officer,

c) inform the probation officer about his residence, employment and sources of livelihood, compliance with appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations imposed by the court and other important circumstances for the execution of supervision determined by the probation officer,

d) allow the probation officer entry into the dwelling in which he resides.

Obligations and Powers of the Probation Officer

The probation officer is obliged to exercise supervision over the perpetrator in accordance with the created probation plan, to assist him in his affairs and to carry out instructions given by the presiding judge to the Probation and Mediation Service and which are aimed at the execution of supervision and at ensuring that the perpetrator leads a proper life. The probation officer is obliged to regularly update the probation plan with regard to the results of supervision and to the personal, family and other circumstances of the perpetrator.

Types of Punishments and Exceptional Punishment

Types of Punishments

(1) For committed criminal offenses, the court may impose punishments

h) prohibition on keeping and breeding animals,

j) prohibition on entry to sports, cultural and other social events,

k) loss of honorary titles or decorations,

(2) Imprisonment means, unless the criminal law provides otherwise,

a) unconditional sentence of imprisonment,

b) conditional sentence of imprisonment,

c) conditional sentence of imprisonment with supervision.

(3) A special type of imprisonment is exceptional punishment (§ 54).

Imposition of Multiple Punishments Independently and Concurrently

(1) If the criminal law provides several punishments for a criminal offense, each such punishment may be imposed independently or several of these punishments concurrently. In addition to the punishment provided by the criminal law for a criminal offense, other punishments listed in § 52 may also be imposed. However, house arrest may not be imposed concurrently with imprisonment and community service, community service concurrently with imprisonment, a monetary penalty concurrently with forfeiture of property, and prohibition of residence concurrently with expulsion.

(2) House arrest, community service, monetary penalty, prohibition on keeping and breeding animals, prohibition on entry to sports, cultural and other social events, expulsion and prohibition of residence may be imposed independently, even if the criminal law does not provide such punishment for a criminal offense.

Exceptional Punishment

(1) Exceptional punishment means both a sentence of imprisonment of over twenty up to thirty years and a sentence of imprisonment for life. Exceptional punishment may be imposed only for a particularly serious felony for which the criminal law permits it.

(2) A sentence of imprisonment of over twenty up to thirty years may be imposed by the court only if the seriousness of the particularly serious felony is very high or the possibility of reforming the perpetrator is particularly difficult.

(3) A sentence of imprisonment for life may be imposed by the court only on a perpetrator who committed a particularly serious felony of murder under § 140 paragraph 3, or who in committing a particularly serious felony of public endangerment under § 272 paragraph 3, high treason (§ 309), terrorist attack under § 311 paragraph 3, terror (§ 312), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), use of a prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat under § 411 paragraph 3, war cruelty under § 412 paragraph 3, persecution of population under § 413 paragraph 3 or abuse of internationally recognized and state symbols under § 415 paragraph 3 intentionally caused the death of another person, provided that

a) such a particularly serious felony is extraordinarily serious having regard to a particularly reprehensible manner of committing the act or to a particularly reprehensible motive or to a particularly grave and difficult to remedy consequence, and

b) the imposition of such punishment is required for effective protection of society or there is no hope that the perpetrator could be reformed by a sentence of imprisonment of over twenty up to thirty years.

(4) If the court imposes a sentence of imprisonment for life, it may at the same time decide that the period of serving the sentence in a prison with increased security shall not be counted toward the period of serving the sentence for the purposes of conditional release.

Imposition and Execution of Individual Punishments

Imprisonment

(1) An unconditional sentence of imprisonment is imposed for a maximum of twenty years, unless it concerns an extraordinary increase of the sentence of imprisonment (§ 59), imposition of a sentence of imprisonment on the perpetrator of a criminal offense committed for the benefit of an organized criminal group (§ 108) or an exceptional punishment (§ 54).

(2) For criminal offenses where the upper limit of the sentence of imprisonment does not exceed five years, an unconditional sentence of imprisonment may be imposed only on condition that, having regard to the person of the perpetrator, the imposition of another punishment would manifestly not lead to the perpetrator leading a proper life. For the criminal offense of neglect of compulsory maintenance under § 196 paragraph 1 or 2, an unconditional sentence of imprisonment may be imposed only on condition that the imposition of such punishment is required for effective protection of society or there is no hope that the perpetrator could be reformed by another punishment.

(3) An unconditional sentence of imprisonment is executed according to another legal regulation in prisons.

Execution of Sentence of Imprisonment

(1) An unconditional sentence of imprisonment is executed in a differentiated manner in a prison

(2) The court shall as a rule place in a prison

a) with security a perpetrator who does not meet the conditions for placement in a prison with increased security,

b) with increased security a perpetrator on whom an exceptional punishment (§ 54) has been imposed, on whom a sentence of imprisonment for a criminal offense committed for the benefit of an organized criminal group (§ 108) has been imposed, on whom a sentence of imprisonment of at least eight years for a particularly serious felony (§ 14 paragraph 3) has been imposed, or who was convicted of an intentional criminal offense and in the last five years escaped or attempted to escape from custody, from serving a sentence or from serving preventive detention.

(3) The court may place the perpetrator in a prison of a different type than that into which he is to be placed according to paragraph 2, if, having regard to the seriousness of the criminal offense and to the degree and nature of the perpetrator's disturbance, it considers that influencing him to lead a proper life will be better ensured in a different type of prison; however, it shall always place in a prison with increased security a perpetrator on whom a sentence of imprisonment for life has been imposed.

(4) The manner of executing the sentence in individual types of prisons is regulated by another legal regulation.

Reassignment of a Convicted Person to a Prison of Another Type

(1) During the execution of a sentence of imprisonment, the court may decide on the reassignment of a convicted person to a prison of another type.

(2) The court shall decide on the reassignment of a convicted person to a prison with security if the convicted person's behavior and the manner in which he fulfills his obligations justify the conclusion that the reassignment will contribute to his reform.

(3) The court may decide on the reassignment of a convicted person to a prison with increased security if

a) the convicted person seriously or repeatedly violated the established order or discipline, or

b) the convicted person was found guilty by a final judgment of a criminal offense committed during the execution of the sentence.

(4) It is not possible to reassign from a prison with increased security

a) a convicted person on whom a sentence of imprisonment for life has been imposed and who has not yet served at least ten years of this sentence,

b) another convicted person who is serving the sentence in a prison with increased security, before serving at least one quarter of the imposed sentence.

(5) Upon the proposal of a convicted person who in a prison with increased security has continuously served at least one quarter of the imposed sentence, but at least six months, the court may decide on his reassignment to a prison with security. A convicted person on whom a sentence of imprisonment for life has been imposed may submit a proposal for reassignment to a prison with security no earlier than after serving ten years of this sentence.

(6) If the proposal according to paragraph 5 is not granted, the convicted person may repeat it only after the expiry of six months from the end of the proceedings on his previous proposal.

## § 57 revoked

Extraordinary Reduction of Imprisonment

(1) If the court, having regard to the circumstances of the case or the circumstances of the offender, considers that the application of the sentence of imprisonment provided by the Criminal Code would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that the rehabilitation of the offender can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration, it may reduce the sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range provided by this Act. (2) If the court considers that, having regard to the circumstances of the offender and the nature of the criminal activity committed by him, his rehabilitation can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration, it may reduce the sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where it sentences an offender who

a) helped to prevent a criminal offence which another person was preparing or attempting to commit, or

(3) If it can be considered that, having regard to the circumstances of the offender and the nature of the criminal activity committed by him, his rehabilitation can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration, a sentence of imprisonment may be imposed below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where it is imposed by a judgment approving a plea agreement on guilt and punishment. (4) In reducing a sentence of imprisonment pursuant to paragraphs 1 to 3, a sentence may not be imposed

a) below five years, if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least twelve years,

b) below three years, if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least eight years,

c) below one year, if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least five years. (5) The court shall reduce the sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range also for an offender designated as a cooperating accused, if the conditions set out in Section 178a(1) of the Code of Criminal Procedure are met and the cooperating accused has given, both in the preparatory proceedings and in the proceedings before the court, a complete and truthful testimony concerning facts that are capable of significantly contributing to the clarification of a felony committed by members of an organized group, in connection with an organized group or for the benefit of an organized criminal group; in doing so, it shall take into account the nature of the criminal offence mentioned in his confession in comparison with the felony committed by members of an organized group, in connection with an organized group or for the benefit of an organized criminal group, to the clarification of which he contributed, as well as the significance of such action by him, the person of the offender and the circumstances of the case, in particular whether and in what manner he participated in such felony to the clarification of which he committed himself, and what consequences he possibly caused by his actions. It is not bound by the limitation provided in paragraph 4. (6) The court may reduce the sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where it sentences an offender for preparation for a criminal offence or for an attempt to commit a criminal offence or for aiding and abetting a criminal offence and, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the preparation or attempt or aiding and abetting, considers that the application of the sentence of imprisonment provided by this Act would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that the rehabilitation of the offender can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration. It is not bound by the limitation provided in paragraph 4. (7) The court may reduce the sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where the offender acted in legal error but could have avoided such error (Section 19(2)), committed a criminal offence averting an attack or other danger without the conditions of extreme necessity (Section 28) or necessary defence (Section 29) being fully met, or exceeded the limits of permissible risk (Section 31) or the limits of another circumstance excluding unlawfulness. It is not bound by the limitation provided in paragraph 4. Extraordinary Increase of Imprisonment

(1) For an offender who has again committed a particularly serious felony (Section 14(3)) even though he has already been punished for such or another particularly serious felony, the court may impose a sentence in the upper half of the sentencing range of imprisonment provided in the Criminal Code, the upper limit of which is increased by one third, if the seriousness of the particularly serious felony is high having regard to such recidivism and other circumstances of the case or the possibility of rehabilitation of the offender is aggravated. (2) The upper limit of the sentencing range for imprisonment may, after an increase pursuant to paragraph 1, exceed twenty years. When imposing an exceptional sentence of imprisonment of more than twenty years up to thirty years, the upper limit must not exceed thirty years. House Arrest

(1) The court may impose a sentence of house arrest of up to two years if it sentences an offender for a misdemeanour, if

a) having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, there are reasonable grounds to believe that the imposition of this sentence, possibly in addition to another sentence, will suffice, and

b) the offender gives a written promise that he will stay in the dwelling or a part thereof at a specified address during the specified period and will provide all necessary cooperation during the execution of supervision. (2) As an independent sentence, a sentence of house arrest may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of house arrest consists in the convicted person's obligation to stay, during the execution of this sentence, in a specified dwelling or a part thereof during a time period determined by the court, unless prevented from doing so by important reasons, in particular the performance of employment or profession or the provision of health services by a health service provider as a result of his illness or injury; the health service provider is obliged to communicate this fact to the criminal proceedings authority upon request. (4) The court shall determine the time period during which the convicted person is obliged to stay in the specified dwelling or a part thereof, on working days and on days of rest from work and time off from work, having regard in particular to his working hours and the time necessary to travel to work, to care for minor children and to handle necessary personal and family matters, so as to deprive him appropriately of liberty while ensuring all necessary needs of the convicted person and his family. The court may permit the convicted person to attend regular religious services or religious gatherings also on days of rest from work and time off from work. (5) The court may impose supervision over the offender for the duration of the sentence of house arrest; Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (6) The court may impose on the offender for the duration of this sentence appropriate restrictions or appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to compensate, according to his means, for damage or to redress non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (7) If the offender is of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures specified in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions set out for juveniles. Cancellation of Supervision or Appropriate Restrictions, Appropriate Duties and Educational Measures

If a convicted person to whom a sentence of house arrest has been imposed leads a proper life and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may cancel the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate duty or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them, or may cancel the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and supervise his behaviour to an increased extent; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the execution of one third of the imposed sentence of house arrest, but at least after six months of execution of this sentence. Substitute Imprisonment

(1) When imposing a sentence of house arrest, the court shall determine, for the event that the offender would evade commencement of the execution of this sentence, would without serious reason breach the agreed conditions of its execution, would culpably fail to execute the imposed sentence in the specified time period or would otherwise frustrate the execution of this sentence or would not lead a proper life, a substitute sentence of imprisonment of up to one year; the substitute sentence of imprisonment must not exceed the upper limit of the sentencing range for imprisonment. The court may decide on the execution of the substitute sentence of imprisonment also during the period specified for the execution of the sentence of house arrest; if it decides on the execution of the substitute sentence of imprisonment, it shall also determine the manner of its execution. (2) If the convicted person has given cause for a decision that the substitute sentence of imprisonment shall be executed after the execution of half of the imposed sentence of house arrest, the court shall reduce the length of the substitute sentence of imprisonment determined pursuant to paragraph 1 to one half. (3) Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, keep the sentence of house arrest in force even though the convicted person has given cause for a decision on the execution of the substitute sentence of imprisonment, and

a) impose supervision over the convicted person for the period of execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof,

b) extend the period of execution of this sentence by up to one year, provided that it does not exceed the maximum term of this sentence specified in Section 60(1),

c) impose on the convicted person for the period of execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof an appropriate restriction or appropriate duty pursuant to Section 48(4) not yet imposed, aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life, or

d) impose on the convicted person for the period of execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof some of the educational measures pursuant to Section 60(7), if he is of an age close to the age of juveniles. (4) Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. Community Service

(1) The court may impose a sentence of community service if it sentences an offender for a misdemeanour; as an independent sentence, a sentence of community service may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (2) The court shall generally not impose a sentence of community service in the case of an offender for whom a sentence of community service was converted into a sentence of imprisonment pursuant to Section 65(3) during the three years preceding the imposition of this type of sentence. (3) A sentence of community service consists in the convicted person's obligation to perform work for generally beneficial purposes to the extent determined in favour of a provider of community service, which may be the state, a region, a municipality or a legal entity engaged in education and science, culture, schooling, health protection, fire protection, environmental protection, support and protection of youth, animal protection, humanitarian, social, charitable, religious, physical education and sports activities and which performs such activities for a publicly beneficial purpose. The work must not serve the gainful purposes of the convicted person. Term of the Sentence of Community Service

(1) The court may impose a sentence of community service in the term from 50 to 300 hours. (2) The court may impose on the offender for the period of the sentence also appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to compensate, according to his means, for damage or to redress non-pecuniary harm caused by the misdemeanour, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the misdemeanour. (3) If the offender is of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures specified in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions set out for juveniles. Position of the Offender and His Health Fitness

When imposing a sentence of community service, the court shall take into account the position of the offender, his state of health and the possibility of imposing this sentence. It shall not impose a sentence of community service if the offender is not fit for continuous performance of work for health reasons. Execution of the Sentence of Community Service

(1) The convicted person is obliged to perform the community service personally and free of charge in his free time no later than one year from the day determined as the day of commencement of the execution of this sentence. This period shall not include the time during which the convicted person

a) could not perform the community service due to health or legal obstacles, or

b) was in custody or was serving a sentence of imprisonment. (2) If a convicted person to whom a sentence of community service has been imposed leads a proper life and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may cancel the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate duty or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them; the court may make this decision at the earliest after six months from the day determined as the day of commencement of the execution of this sentence. (3) If the offender does not lead a proper life from the time of sentencing to the end of the execution of the sentence of community service, evades commencement of the execution of the sentence, without serious reason breaches the agreed conditions of the execution of the sentence of community service, otherwise frustrates the execution of this sentence or culpably fails to execute this sentence within the specified period, the court may convert, also during the period specified for its execution, the sentence of community service or the remainder thereof into a sentence of imprisonment and decide simultaneously on the manner of its execution; for this purpose, each hour, even only begun, of unexecuted sentence of community service shall count as one day of imprisonment.
