(4) Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, keep the sentence of community service in force or extend the period of execution of this sentence by up to six months, even though the convicted person has given cause for the conversion of the sentence pursuant to paragraph 3, and

a) impose supervision over the convicted person for the period of execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof,

b) impose on the convicted person for the period of execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof appropriate restrictions or appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4) not yet imposed, or

c) impose on the convicted person for the period of execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof some of the educational measures pursuant to Section 63(3), if he is of an age close to the age of juveniles. Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (5) An offender to whom a sentence of community service has been imposed shall be regarded as not having been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed or execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof has been validly waived. Forfeiture of Property

(1) The court may, having regard to the circumstances of the criminal offence committed and the circumstances of the offender, impose a sentence of forfeiture of property if it sentences the offender to an exceptional sentence or if it sentences him for a particularly serious felony by which the offender obtained or attempted to obtain pecuniary gain for himself or another. (2) Without the conditions of paragraph 1, the court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of property only if the Criminal Code permits the imposition of this sentence for the felony committed; as an independent sentence, a sentence of forfeiture of property may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) Forfeiture of property affects the entire property of the convicted person or that part thereof which the court determines; however, the forfeiture does not extend to means or things which are necessary to satisfy the vital needs of the convicted person or of persons whose maintenance or upbringing the convicted person is obliged to provide for by law. (4) The pronouncement of forfeiture of property terminates the community of property of spouses. (5) Forfeited property falls to the state. Liens on forfeited property do not cease to exist. Pecuniary Penalty

(1) The court may impose a pecuniary penalty if the offender obtained or attempted to obtain pecuniary gain for himself or another by an intentional criminal offence. (2) Without the conditions of paragraph 1, the court may impose a pecuniary penalty only if

a) the Criminal Code permits the imposition of this sentence for the criminal offence committed, or

b) it imposes it for a misdemeanour and, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, it does not impose an unconditional sentence of imprisonment at the same time. (3) As an independent sentence, a pecuniary penalty may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. Term of the Pecuniary Penalty

(1) A pecuniary penalty is imposed in daily rates and amounts to at least 20 and at most 730 whole daily rates. (2) A daily rate amounts to at least CZK 100 and at most CZK 50,000. (3) The court shall determine the number of daily rates having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed. However, twice the number of daily rates must not, even together with an imposed sentence of imprisonment and a substitute sentence of imprisonment for a sentence of house arrest, exceed the upper limit of the sentencing range for imprisonment. The court shall determine the amount of one daily rate of the pecuniary penalty with regard to the personal and property circumstances of the offender. In doing so, it shall generally proceed from the net income which the offender has or could have on average per one day. (4) The income of the offender, his property and proceeds therefrom, as well as other bases for determining the amount of the daily rate, may be determined by the court's estimate. (5) The court shall state the number and amount of daily rates in the decision. With regard to the personal and property circumstances of the offender, the court may determine that the pecuniary penalty will be paid in appropriate monthly instalments; in doing so, it may determine that the benefit of instalments of the pecuniary penalty shall lapse if the offender fails to pay a partial instalment on time. (6) The court shall not impose a pecuniary penalty if it is evident that it would be uncollectible. Execution of the Pecuniary Penalty

(1) Paid amounts of the pecuniary penalty fall to the state. (2) If the offender fails to pay the pecuniary penalty upon summons by the court or by the expiry of the period for which execution of the sentence was postponed or for which instalments were permitted, and if it is evident that enforcement of the pecuniary penalty could be frustrated or would be unsuccessful, the court shall convert the pecuniary penalty or the remainder thereof into a sentence of imprisonment and decide simultaneously on the manner of its execution; for this purpose, each amount corresponding to one daily rate not paid in full shall count as two days of imprisonment. (3) An offender to whom a pecuniary penalty has been imposed for a criminal offence other than a particularly serious felony shall be regarded as not having been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed or execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof has been validly waived. Forfeiture of a Thing

(1) The court shall impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing which is a direct proceeds from criminal activity. (2) The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing,

a) which is an instrument of criminal activity, or

b) which is an indirect proceeds from criminal activity, if the value of the thing constituting the direct proceeds from criminal activity is not negligible in relation to the value of the thing constituting the indirect proceeds from criminal activity. (3) The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing only if it is a thing belonging to the offender. (4) If the offender holds in breach of another legal regulation a thing specified in paragraph 2, in relation to which it is possible to impose forfeiture of a thing, the court shall always also impose this sentence on him. (5) Before the legal effect of the decision, a prohibition of alienation of the forfeited thing applies, which also includes a prohibition of other dispositions aimed at frustrating the sentence of forfeiture of a thing. (6) A forfeited thing falls to the state. Liens on a forfeited thing do not cease to exist. Forfeiture of Substitute Value

(1) If the offender, before the imposition of the sentence of forfeiture of a thing, destroys, damages or otherwise depreciates, alienates, renders unusable, removes or utilises, in particular consumes, a thing which the court could have declared forfeited pursuant to Section 70, or otherwise frustrates its forfeiture, the court may impose on him forfeiture of substitute value up to an amount corresponding to the value of such thing. The court may determine the value of the thing which the court could have declared forfeited on the basis of an expert statement or expert opinion. (2) If a thing is, even if only in part, depreciated, rendered unusable or removed, the court may impose forfeiture of substitute value in addition to forfeiture of a thing pursuant to Section 70. (3) Forfeited substitute value falls to the state. Liens on forfeited substitute value do not cease to exist. Independent Sentence of Forfeiture of a Thing

The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing as an independent sentence only if the Criminal Code permits the imposition of this sentence and if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. Prohibition of Activity

(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of activity for one year to ten years if the offender committed a criminal offence in connection with this activity. (2) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of activity as an independent sentence only if the Criminal Code permits the imposition of this sentence for the criminal offence committed and if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of prohibition of activity consists in prohibiting the convicted person, for the period of execution of this sentence, from exercising a certain employment, profession or function or such activity for which a special permit is required, or the exercise of which is regulated by another legal regulation. Execution of the Sentence of Prohibition of Activity

(1) The period of execution of a sentence of imprisonment shall not be counted towards the period of execution of a sentence of prohibition of activity; however, the period during which the offender was, before the legal effect of the judgment, deprived of the authorisation to engage in the activity which is the subject of the prohibition in connection with the criminal offence pursuant to another legal regulation or, on the basis of a measure of a public authority, was no longer allowed to engage in this activity or could not engage in it because he committed himself to that for the purposes of conditional deferral of filing a motion for punishment or conditional suspension of criminal prosecution, shall be counted. (2) If the sentence of prohibition of activity has been executed, the offender shall be regarded as not having been convicted. Prohibition of Keeping and Breeding Animals

(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals for up to ten years if the offender committed a criminal offence in connection with keeping, breeding or caring for an animal. (2) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals as an independent sentence only if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals consists in prohibiting the convicted person, for the period of execution of this sentence, from keeping, breeding and caring for an animal. Execution of the Sentence of Prohibition of Keeping and Breeding Animals

(1) The period of execution of a sentence of imprisonment shall not be counted towards the period of execution of a sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals; however, the period during which the offender, before the legal effect of the judgment, on the basis of a decision or measure of a public authority, was no longer allowed to keep or breed the animals which are the subject of the prohibition or care for them or could not keep or breed them or care for them because he committed himself to that for the purposes of conditional deferral of filing a motion for punishment or conditional suspension of criminal prosecution, shall be counted. (2) If the sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals has been executed, the offender shall be regarded as not having been convicted. Prohibition of Residence

(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of residence for one year to ten years for an intentional criminal offence if, with regard to the offender's previous way of life and the place of commission of the act, the protection of public order, family, health, morals or property so requires; the sentence of prohibition of residence must not relate to the place or district in which the offender has permanent residence. (2) A sentence of prohibition of residence may be imposed as an independent sentence for a criminal offence for which the Criminal Code provides a sentence of imprisonment the upper limit of the sentencing range of which does not exceed three years, if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) The court may impose on the offender for the period of execution of this sentence appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to compensate, according to his means, for damage or to redress non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (4) If the offender is of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures specified in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions set out for juveniles. (5) The period of execution of a sentence of imprisonment shall not be counted towards the period of execution of a sentence of prohibition of residence; the court shall make a decision pursuant to paragraphs 3 and 4 only after the execution of the sentence of imprisonment or after conditional release. (6) If a convicted person to whom a sentence of prohibition of residence has been imposed leads a proper life during its execution and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may cancel the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate duty or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the execution of one third of the imposed sentence of prohibition of residence, but at least after six months of execution of this sentence. (7) A sentence of prohibition of residence consists in the convicted person not being allowed, during the execution of this sentence, to stay in a specified place or in a specified district; for temporary stay in such place or in such district in necessary personal matters, permission is required. Prohibition of Entry to Sporting, Cultural and Other Social Events

(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events for up to ten years if the offender committed an intentional criminal offence in connection with attending such an event. (2) As an independent sentence, a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events consists in prohibiting the convicted person, for the period of execution of this sentence, from attending specified sporting, cultural and other social events. Execution of the Sentence of Prohibition of Entry to Sporting, Cultural and Other Social Events

(1) During the execution of a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events, the convicted person is obliged to cooperate with the probation officer in the manner he determines, in particular to proceed according to the established probation plan, to perform established programmes of social training and re-education, programmes of psychological counselling, and if the probation officer considers it necessary, to report according to his instructions in the period immediately related to the holding of the prohibited event to a specified unit of the Police of the Czech Republic. (2) The period of execution of a sentence of imprisonment shall not be counted towards the period of execution of a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events. Loss of Honorary Titles or Decorations

(1) The court may impose a sentence of loss of honorary titles or decorations if it sentences the offender for an intentional criminal offence committed from a particularly reprehensible motive to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment of at least two years. (2) Loss of honorary titles or decorations consists in the convicted person losing decorations, honorary recognitions and other honorary titles awarded pursuant to domestic legal regulations. Loss of Military Rank

(1) The court may impose a sentence of loss of military rank if it sentences the offender for an intentional criminal offence committed from a particularly reprehensible motive to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment of at least two years. (2) The court may also impose this sentence in addition to another sentence if, having regard to the nature of the criminal offence committed, discipline and order in the armed forces so require. (3) Loss of military rank consists in the convicted person's rank in the armed forces being reduced to the rank of private. Expulsion

(1) The court may impose on an offender who is not a citizen of the Czech Republic a sentence of expulsion from the territory of the Czech Republic, either as an independent sentence or in addition to another sentence, if the safety of people or property or another general interest so requires; as an independent sentence, a sentence of expulsion may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (2) Having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed, the possibilities of rehabilitation and the circumstances of the offender and the degree of threat to the safety of people, property or another general interest, the court may impose a sentence of expulsion in the term from one year to ten years, or for an indefinite period. (3) The court shall not impose a sentence of expulsion if

a) it was not possible to ascertain the nationality of the offender,

b) the offender was granted asylum pursuant to another legal regulation,

c) its consequence would be a disproportionate interference with the private or family life of the offender,

d) there is a danger that the offender will be persecuted in the state to which he should be expelled for his race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, membership of a certain social group, political or religious conviction, or if expulsion would expose the offender to torture or other inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment,

e) the offender is a citizen of the European Union or a family member thereof regardless of nationality and has permanent residence permitted on the territory of the Czech Republic or is a foreigner with the legal status of a long-term resident on the territory of the Czech Republic granted pursuant to another legal regulation, unless it finds serious grounds for threat to state security or public order,

f) the offender is a citizen of the European Union and has continuously resided on the territory of the Czech Republic for the last 10 years, unless it finds imperative grounds for threat to state security, or

g) the offender is a child who is a citizen of the European Union, unless expulsion would be in his best interests. Conditional Sentencing to Imprisonment

Conditional Suspension of the Execution of Imprisonment

(1) The court may conditionally suspend the execution of a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding three years if, having regard to the person and circumstances of the offender, in particular with regard to his previous life and the environment in which he lives and works, and to the circumstances of the case, it has reasonable grounds to believe that its execution is not necessary to influence the offender to lead a proper life. (2) The permission of conditional suspension of the execution of imprisonment does not apply to the execution of other sentences imposed in addition to this sentence. Probationary Period, Appropriate Restrictions and Appropriate Duties

(1) In conditional sentencing, the court shall determine a probationary period of one year to five years, but not for a period shorter than the length of the conditionally suspended sentence of imprisonment; the probationary period commences upon the legal effect of the judgment. (2) In conditional sentencing, the court may impose on the offender the obligation to stay, in a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, in a specified time period in a specified dwelling or a part thereof. The total duration of the stay of the conditionally sentenced person in a specified dwelling or a part thereof must not exceed one year, even in the case of determination of a longer probationary period. (3) The court may impose on the conditionally sentenced person appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to compensate, according to his means, for damage or to redress non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (4) If the offender is of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to appropriate restrictions and appropriate duties specified in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures specified in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions set out for juveniles. (5) The period during which the conditionally sentenced person led a proper life in the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions shall be counted towards the probationary period newly determined in conditional sentencing for the same act or towards the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate or cumulative sentence or a joint sentence for continuation of a criminal offence; if the court again imposes the obligation to stay in a specified time period in a specified dwelling or a part thereof, the period during which the convicted person properly performed this obligation shall be counted towards the duration of this obligation. Cancellation of Appropriate Restrictions, Appropriate Duties and Educational Measures

If a conditionally sentenced person leads a proper life and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may cancel the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate duty or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them; the court may make this decision at the earliest after one third of the probationary period has elapsed, but at least after six months of its duration. The determined probationary period remains unaffected by the decision pursuant to the first sentence. Decision on Conditional Sentencing

(1) If the conditionally sentenced person led a proper life in the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the sentence shall be executed. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, keep the conditional sentencing in force even though the convicted person has given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and

a) impose supervision over the convicted person,

b) extend the probationary period appropriately, but not by more than two years, provided that it does not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period specified in Section 82(1),1,

c) impose reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations not yet imposed as referred to in § 48 para. 4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life,

d) impose educational measures not yet imposed pursuant to § 82 para. 4, if he is of an age close to that of juveniles, or

e) order that the conditionally convicted person stay in a designated dwelling or part thereof pursuant to § 82 para. 2. (2) The provisions of § 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (3) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 1 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period, without the conditionally convicted person being at fault, the conditionally convicted person shall be deemed to have proven himself. (4) Where it has been declared that the conditionally convicted person has proven himself, or where he is deemed to have proven himself, the offender shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. (5) If the court decides pursuant to paragraph 1 that the sentence is to be executed, it shall at the same time decide on the manner of execution of the sentence. Conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence with supervision

Where it is necessary to monitor and control the offender's behaviour to an increased extent and to provide him with necessary care and assistance during the probationary period, the court may, under the conditions set out in § 81 para. 1, conditionally suspend the execution of a custodial sentence not exceeding three years while declaring supervision over the offender. The provisions of § 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. Probationary period, reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations

(1) In case of conditional conviction with supervision, the court shall set a probationary period of one to five years, but not for a period shorter than the length of the conditionally suspended custodial sentence; the probationary period shall commence upon the judgment becoming final. (2) In case of conditional conviction with supervision, the court may order the offender to stay, during a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, in a designated time period in a designated dwelling or part thereof. The total duration of the conditionally convicted person's stay in the designated dwelling or part thereof may not exceed one year, even where a longer probationary period is set. (3) The court may impose on the conditionally convicted person under supervision reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations as referred to in § 48 para. 4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; it shall usually also order him to make reparation for damage or non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence to the extent of his abilities, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (4) Where the offender is of an age close to that of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to reasonable restrictions or reasonable obligations referred to in § 48 para. 4, also some of the educational measures set out in the Act on Juvenile Justice by analogous application of the conditions established for juveniles. Revocation of supervision or reasonable restrictions, reasonable obligations and educational measures

Where the conditionally convicted person who has been placed under supervision leads a proper life during the probationary period and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life without them, or may revoke the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his behaviour to an increased extent; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the expiry of one third of the imposed probationary period, but at least after six months of its duration. The established probationary period shall remain unaffected by the decision pursuant to the first sentence. Decision on conditional conviction with supervision

(1) Where the conditionally convicted person with respect to whom supervision has been declared led a proper life during the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proven himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the sentence is to be executed. Exceptionally, the court may, with regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, maintain the conditional conviction with supervision, even though the convicted person has given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and

a) establish further obligations over the convicted person within the framework of the imposed supervision,

b) reasonably extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, and may not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period established in § 85 para. 1,

c) impose reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations not yet imposed as referred to in § 48 para. 4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life,

d) impose educational measures not yet imposed pursuant to § 85 para. 4, if he is of an age close to that of juveniles, or

e) order that the conditionally convicted person stay in a designated dwelling or part thereof pursuant to § 85 para. 2. (2) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 1 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period, without the conditionally convicted person being at fault, the conditionally convicted person shall be deemed to have proven himself. (3) Where it has been declared that the conditionally convicted person has proven himself, or where he is deemed to have proven himself, the offender shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. (4) If the court decides pursuant to paragraph 1 that the sentence is to be executed, it shall at the same time decide on the manner of execution of the sentence. Analogous application of provisions on conditional conviction

The provisions on conditional conviction shall apply mutatis mutandis to the relationship of conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence to other sentences imposed alongside this sentence pursuant to § 81 para. 2 and to the offsetting of the period during which the conditionally convicted person with supervision led a proper life during the probationary period or properly performed the obligation to stay in a designated time period in a designated dwelling or part thereof, into a new probationary period or into the duration of this obligation if it is imposed again, pursuant to § 82 para. 5. Conditional release from execution of custodial sentence and conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events

Conditional release from execution of custodial sentence

(1) The court may conditionally release a convicted person to freedom if the convicted person, after the judgment becomes final, particularly during the execution of the sentence by his behaviour and fulfilment of his obligations has demonstrated improvement and it can be expected that in the future he will lead a proper life, or if the court accepts a guarantee for completion of the convicted person's reform, and

a) the convicted person has served at least half of the imposed custodial sentence or custodial sentence mitigated by decision of the President of the Czech Republic, or

b) the convicted person who has not been convicted of a particularly serious felony,

1. by which death was or was to be caused, unless it concerns the criminal offence of killing pursuant to § 141 para. 1,

2. by which serious bodily harm was or was to be caused,

3. the element of which is commission against a pregnant woman or child,

4. which he committed as a member of an organised group, in connection with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group, or the particularly serious felony of participation in an organised criminal group (§ 361),

5. which he committed with the intention of enabling or facilitating the commission of a terrorist criminal offence, the criminal offence of participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism pursuant to § 312e para. 3 or threatening with a terrorist criminal offence (§ 312f), or

6. which is set out in Part Three, Seven, Nine, Twelve and Thirteen of the Special Part of this Act or in paragraph 4, and who has not yet been in execution of custodial sentence, has served at least one third of the imposed custodial sentence or custodial sentence mitigated by decision of the President of the Czech Republic. (2) Where a convicted person for a misdemeanour has demonstrated by his exemplary behaviour and fulfilment of his obligations that further execution of the sentence is not necessary, the court may conditionally release him to freedom even before he has served the part of the custodial sentence required for conditional release pursuant to paragraph 1. The court shall not grant a proposal by the prison director for conditional release of a convicted person to freedom only if it is obvious that the convicted person would not lead a proper life after release to freedom. (3) When deciding on conditional release of a convicted person for a felony, the court shall also take into account whether the convicted person commenced the execution of the sentence in due time and whether he partially or fully made reparation or otherwise redressed the damage or other harm caused by the criminal offence or whether he surrendered unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. Where the convicted person underwent protective treatment before commencing the execution of the custodial sentence or during its course, the court shall also take into account the convicted person's demonstrated attitude to the execution of protective treatment. (4) A person convicted of the criminal offence of murder (§ 140), killing pursuant to § 141 para. 2, grievous bodily harm pursuant to § 145 para. 3, torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment pursuant to § 149 para. 4, unlawful termination of pregnancy without the pregnant woman's consent pursuant to § 159 para. 3, 4, unauthorised removal of tissues and organs pursuant to § 164 para. 3, 4, trafficking in human beings pursuant to § 168 para. 4, 5, deprivation of personal liberty pursuant to § 170 para. 2, 3, abduction pursuant to § 172 para. 2, 3, robbery pursuant to § 173 para. 3, 4, hostage taking pursuant to § 174 para. 3, 4, extortion pursuant to § 175 para. 3, 4, rape pursuant to § 185 para. 3, 4, sexual abuse pursuant to § 187 para. 3, 4, public endangerment pursuant to § 272 para. 2, 3, unauthorised production and other handling of narcotic and psychotropic substances and poisons pursuant to § 283 para. 4, seizure of control over an aircraft, civil vessel and fixed platform (§ 290), abduction of an aircraft abroad pursuant to § 292 para. 3, high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), sabotage (§ 314), espionage pursuant to § 316 para. 3, 4, war treason (§ 320), violent crossing of state border pursuant to § 339 para. 3, organising and enabling unlawful crossing of state border pursuant to § 340 para. 4, genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), aggression (§ 405a), preparation of aggressive war (§ 406), contacts endangering peace (§ 409), use of prohibited combat means and unlawful conduct of combat pursuant to § 411 para. 3, war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of population (§ 413), looting in area of war operations (§ 414) or abuse of internationally recognised and state symbols pursuant to § 415 para. 3, as well as a person convicted to an exceptional custodial sentence exceeding twenty up to thirty years (§ 54 para. 2), may be conditionally released only after serving two thirds of the imposed custodial sentence, unless, with regard to the circumstances of the act for which they were convicted and the nature of their personality, there is a threat of repetition of the committed or another similar particularly serious felony. (5) A person convicted to an exceptional custodial sentence of life imprisonment may be conditionally released only after at least twenty years of execution of this sentence, unless, with regard to the circumstances of the act for which they were convicted and the nature of their personality, there is a threat of repetition of the committed or another similar particularly serious felony. Probationary period and reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations in conditional release

(1) In case of conditional release, the court shall set a probationary period for convicted persons for a misdemeanour of up to three years and for convicted persons for a felony of one to seven years; the probationary period shall commence with the conditional release of the convicted person to freedom. The court may simultaneously declare supervision over the offender and at the same time order that during a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, the offender shall stay in a designated time period in a designated dwelling or part thereof. The provisions of § 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (2) The court may impose on the conditionally released person reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations as referred to in § 48 para. 4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; it may also order him to make reparation for damage or non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence to the extent of his abilities, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. Where the conditionally released person is of an age close to that of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in § 48 para. 4, also some of the educational measures set out in the Act on Juvenile Justice by analogous application of the conditions established for juveniles. The court may order a conditionally released person pursuant to § 88 para. 2 to stay, during a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, in a designated time period in a designated dwelling or part thereof, or to perform work for the benefit of providers of community service work, or to pay a specified monetary amount for the benefit of victims of criminal activity to the court's account. (3) The total duration of the designated stay of the conditionally released person in a designated dwelling or part thereof pursuant to paragraphs 1 and 2 may not exceed one year, even where a longer probationary period is set. The performance of work pursuant to paragraph 2 may be established in the amount of 50 to 200 hours. The amount for monetary assistance to victims of criminal activity pursuant to paragraph 2 shall be established in the amount of CZK 2,000 to CZK 10,000,000; when establishing this amount, the court shall also take into account the personal and property circumstances of the convicted person and with regard thereto may establish that the specified amount will be paid in reasonable monthly instalments. (4) Where the conditionally released person leads a proper life during the probationary period and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life without them, or may revoke the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his behaviour to an increased extent; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the expiry of one third of the imposed probationary period, but at least after six months of its duration. The established probationary period shall remain unaffected by the decision pursuant to the first sentence. Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events

(1) After execution of half of the sentence of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person during the execution of the sentence by his way of life has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is not necessary, or if the court accepts a guarantee for completion of the convicted person's reform. When deciding on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, the court may take into account whether the convicted person has successfully undergone a suitable social training and re-education programme or psychological counselling focused on the activity which is the subject of the prohibition. (2) In case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events, the court shall set a probationary period of up to five years, but not for a period shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period shall commence upon the decision on this waiver becoming final. Common provision

(1) Where the conditionally released person or a convicted person with respect to whom conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events has been made led a proper life during the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proven himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence is to be executed. Exceptionally, the court may, with regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, maintain the conditional release or conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events, even though the convicted person has given cause for a decision that the remainder of the sentence is to be executed, and

a) establish supervision over the convicted person,

b) reasonably extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, and may not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period established in § 89 para. 1 or § 90 para. 2, or

c) impose reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations not yet imposed as referred to in § 48 para. 4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life. (2) The provisions of § 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (3) Where the court has declared that the conditionally released person or a convicted person with respect to whom conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events has been made has proven himself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when he was conditionally released or when the decision that conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events is made became final. (4) With respect to a conditionally released person or a convicted person with respect to whom conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events has been made, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the conditional release occurred or when the decision that conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events is made became final, also where the court has not made, without his fault, a decision pursuant to paragraph 1 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period. (5) Repeated conditional release from execution of the same sentence is possible after execution of half of the remainder of the sentence and in cases referred to in § 88 para. 4 after execution of two thirds of the remainder of the sentence. Repeated conditional release from an exceptional custodial sentence of life imprisonment is not possible. (6) Where the guarantee for completion of the convicted person's reform has been revoked by the person who provided it, the court shall review the convicted person's behaviour during the probationary period, and if it finds that the conditional release or conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events is not fulfilling its purpose, it shall decide that the remainder of the sentence is to be executed; otherwise it shall maintain the conditional release or conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence or prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events. Offsetting custody and sentence

Offsetting custody and sentence

(1) Where criminal proceedings have been conducted against the offender in custody and he is convicted in these proceedings, the period spent in custody shall be offset against the imposed sentence, or aggregate or cumulative sentence, insofar as the offsetting is possible with regard to the type of sentence imposed. (2) Where the offender has been punished by a court or other authority and has been newly convicted for the same act, the executed sentence shall be offset against the imposed sentence, insofar as the offsetting is possible with regard to the type of sentence imposed. The court shall proceed analogously where it has imposed an aggregate or cumulative sentence (§ 43) on the offender or a joint sentence for continuation in a criminal offence (§ 45). (3) Where the offsetting of custody or sentence is not possible, the court shall take this fact into account when establishing the type of sentence, or its extent. (4) The provisions of paragraphs 1 to 3 shall apply analogously to the offsetting of preliminary, extradition and surrender custody executed pursuant to the Act on International Judicial Cooperation in Criminal Matters and to the offsetting of the obligation to stay in a designated time period in a designated dwelling or part thereof imposed on the accused pursuant to § 73 para. 3 of the Code of Criminal Procedure against the sentence imposed for the same act. Offsetting custody and sentence executed abroad

(1) Where the offender has been in custody or punished by an authority of a foreign state or international judicial body abroad for the same act, the period spent in custody or the executed sentence shall be offset against the sentence imposed by a court of the Czech Republic, insofar as the offsetting is possible with regard to the type of sentence imposed. The court shall proceed analogously where it has imposed an aggregate or cumulative sentence (§ 43) on the offender or a joint sentence for continuation in a criminal offence (§ 45). (2) Where the offsetting of custody or sentence imposed abroad (paragraph 1) is not possible, particularly because a sentence of such type that the criminal law does not recognise was executed fully or partially abroad, the court shall take this fact into account when establishing the type of sentence, or its extent. Expiry of sentence execution

Statute of limitations on sentence execution

(1) An imposed sentence cannot be executed after the expiry of the limitation period, which is

a) thirty years, in case of conviction to an exceptional sentence,

b) twenty years, in case of conviction to a custodial sentence exceeding ten years,

c) ten years, in case of conviction to a custodial sentence of at least five years,

d) five years in case of conviction to another sentence. (2) The limitation period shall commence upon the judgment becoming final and in case of conditional conviction or conditional release upon the decision that the sentence is to be executed becoming final. (3) The limitation period shall not include the period during which it was not possible to execute the sentence because the convicted person stayed abroad, underwent institutional protective treatment or preventive detention or another custodial sentence was being executed on him. As regards pecuniary penalty, prohibition from activity, prohibition from keeping and breeding animals, prohibition from residence, prohibition from entering sporting, cultural and other social events and expulsion, the limitation period shall also not include the period during which a custodial sentence was being executed on the convicted person, and in case of expulsion, the period during which the execution of this sentence was suspended. (4) The limitation period shall be interrupted,

a) if the court has taken measures aimed at the execution of the sentence in respect of which limitation is concerned, or

b) if the convicted person has committed a new criminal offence during the limitation period for which the criminal law provides the same or more severe sentence. (5) A new limitation period shall commence upon the interruption of the limitation period. Exclusion from limitation

The execution of a sentence imposed for criminal offences referred to in § 35 is not subject to limitation. Protective measures

General principles for imposing protective measures

Principle of proportionality

(1) A protective measure may not be imposed unless it is proportionate to the nature and seriousness of the act committed by the offender and the danger that the offender poses in the future to interests protected by the criminal law, as well as to the offender's person and his circumstances. (2) The harm caused by an imposed and executed protective measure must not be greater than is necessary to achieve its purpose. Imposing protective measures

(1) Protective measures may be imposed, upon fulfilment of statutory conditions, independently or alongside a sentence. (2) Alongside a sentence of a similar nature, a protective measure may be imposed only if its independent imposition would not be sufficient from the point of view of influence on the person to whom it is imposed and protection of society. (3) Where conditions for imposing several protective measures are fulfilled, they may be imposed alongside each other, unless the criminal law provides otherwise. However, if the necessary influence on the person to whom it is imposed and adequate protection of society can be achieved by only one of them, only such protective measure shall be imposed. (4) Where several protective measures are imposed alongside each other which cannot be executed simultaneously, the court shall establish the order of their execution. Protective measures and their imposition

Types of protective measures

(1) Protective measures are protective treatment, preventive detention, confiscation of thing, confiscation of part of assets and protective upbringing. (2) The imposition of protective upbringing is regulated by the Act on Juvenile Justice. (3) Protective treatment may not be imposed alongside preventive detention. Confiscation of part of assets may not be imposed alongside forfeiture of the same part of assets. Protective treatment

(1) The court shall impose protective treatment in cases referred to in § 40 para. 2 and § 47 para. 1, or if the offender of an otherwise criminal act is not criminally liable due to insanity and his stay at liberty is dangerous. (2) The court may impose protective treatment also where

a) the offender committed a criminal offence in a state induced by a mental disorder and his stay at liberty is dangerous, or

b) the offender who abuses an addictive substance committed a criminal offence under its influence or in connection with its abuse; however, protective treatment shall not be imposed if, with regard to the offender's person, it is obvious that its purpose cannot be achieved. (3) The court may impose protective treatment also alongside a sentence or upon waiver of punishment. (4) According to the nature of the illness and treatment possibilities, the court shall impose institutional or outpatient protective treatment. Where institutional protective treatment has been imposed alongside a custodial sentence, the protective treatment shall be executed as a rule after commencement of execution of the custodial sentence in a prison. Where protective treatment cannot be executed after commencement of execution of the sentence in a prison, institutional protective treatment shall be executed in a medical facility before commencement of execution of the custodial sentence if this better ensures fulfilment of the purpose of treatment, otherwise it shall be executed in a medical facility after execution or other termination of the custodial sentence. Outpatient protective treatment shall be executed as a rule after commencement of execution of the custodial sentence in a prison; where execution of outpatient protective treatment in a prison cannot be carried out, it shall be executed only after execution of the custodial sentence. Where the duration of execution of the custodial sentence in a prison is not sufficient for fulfilment of the purpose of protective treatment, the court may decide on its continuation in a medical facility providing institutional or outpatient care. (5) The court may subsequently change institutional treatment to outpatient treatment and vice versa. The court may change institutional protective treatment to preventive detention under the conditions of § 100 para. 1 or 2. Without the conditions of § 100 para. 1 or 2, the court may change institutional protective treatment to preventive detention if the imposed and executed protective treatment does not fulfil its purpose or does not ensure sufficient protection of society, particularly in cases where the offender has escaped from the medical facility, used violence against employees of the medical facility or other persons during the execution of protective treatment or repeatedly refused examination or treatment procedures. (6) Protective treatment shall continue as long as its purpose requires. Institutional protective treatment shall continue for a maximum of two years; if the treatment is not completed within this period, the court shall, before the expiry of this period, decide on its extension, including repeatedly, always for a maximum of another two years; otherwise it shall decide on release from protective treatment or on change of institutional treatment to outpatient treatment, unless the offender is at fault that the court could not decide within the specified period; in such case the court shall decide without delay after the obstacle preventing the decision has ceased. The duration of protective treatment imposed pursuant to paragraph 2(b) may be terminated as soon as it is determined during its execution that its purpose cannot be achieved; where there is danger that the convicted person will commit another criminal offence, in the decision on release from protective treatment the court shall impose supervision over his behaviour for a period of up to five years; the provisions of § 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. The court shall decide on release from protective treatment. (7) The court shall waive the execution of protective treatment if, before its commencement, the circumstances for which it was imposed cease. Preventive detention

(1) The court shall impose preventive detention in the case referred to in § 47 para. 2, or if the offender of an otherwise criminal act which would meet the elements of a felony is not criminally liable due to insanity, his stay at liberty is dangerous and it cannot be expected that the imposed protective treatment, with regard to the nature of the mental disorder and possibilities of influence on the offender, would lead to sufficient protection of society. (2) The court may impose preventive detention with regard to the offender's person, taking into account his previous life and his circumstances, also where

a) the offender committed a felony in a state induced by a mental disorder, his stay at liberty is dangerous and it cannot be expected that the imposed protective treatment, with regard to the nature of the mental disorder and possibilities of influence on the offender, would lead to sufficient protection of society, or

b) the offender who is addicted to abuse of an addictive substance has again committed a felony, even though he has already been convicted for a felony committed under the influence of an addictive substance or in connection with its abuse to an unconditional custodial sentence of at least two years, and it cannot be expected that sufficient protection of society could be achieved by imposing protective treatment, including with regard to the offender's already demonstrated attitude to protective treatment. (3) The court may impose preventive detention independently, upon waiver of punishment, or also alongside a sentence. Where preventive detention has been imposed alongside an unconditional custodial sentence, it shall be executed after execution or other termination of the custodial sentence. Where an unconditional custodial sentence has been imposed during the execution of preventive detention, its execution shall be suspended for the duration of execution of such custodial sentence. After completion of the execution of the sentence, the execution of preventive detention shall continue. (4) Preventive detention shall be executed in an institution for execution of preventive detention with special security and with therapeutic, psychological, educational, pedagogical, rehabilitation and activity programmes. (5) Preventive detention shall continue as long as the protection of society requires. The court shall review at least once every twelve months and for juveniles once every six months whether the reasons for its further continuation still persist. (6) The court may subsequently change preventive detention to institutional protective treatment if the reasons for which it was imposed cease and the conditions for institutional protective treatment are simultaneously fulfilled. (7) The court shall waive the execution of preventive detention if, before its commencement, the circumstances for which it was imposed cease. Confiscation of thing

(1) Where the sentence of forfeiture of thing referred to in § 70 para. 2(a) has not been imposed, the court may impose that such thing is confiscated,

a) if it belongs to the offender who cannot be prosecuted or convicted,

b) if it belongs to the offender whose punishment the court has waived, or

c) if it endangers the safety of people or property, or society, or there is danger that it will be used for the commission of a felony. (2) The court may impose confiscation of a thing which is immediate proceeds from criminal activity or which is mediated proceeds from criminal activity, provided that the value of the thing constituting immediate proceeds from criminal activity is not negligible in relation to the value of the thing constituting mediated proceeds from criminal activity, and provided that such thing

a) belongs to the offender who has been convicted for the criminal offence from which the thing originates,

b) belongs to the offender who cannot be prosecuted or convicted,

c) belongs to the offender whose punishment the court has waived,

d) belongs to an insane person who committed an otherwise criminal act,

e) belongs to another person to whom the offender transferred such thing or who otherwise acquired it, or

f) forms part of assets in a trust or similar arrangement (hereinafter "trust") or in a mutual fund. (3) Where the offender or another person holds, in violation of another legal regulation, a thing referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, in respect of which confiscation of thing may be imposed, the court shall always impose this protective measure on him. (4) The court may, instead of confiscation of thing, impose an obligation to

a) modify the thing so that it cannot be used for a socially dangerous purpose,

c) remove its marking or make its alteration, and shall establish a reasonable time limit for this purpose. (5) If the obligation established pursuant to paragraph 4 is not fulfilled within the established time limit, the court shall decide on confiscation of thing. Confiscation of substitute value

Where the person from whom a thing could be confiscated pursuant to § 101 para. 1 or 2 destroys, damages or otherwise devalues it, alienates it, renders it unusable, removes it or exploits it, particularly consumes it, or otherwise frustrates its confiscation before a decision on confiscation, or frustrates the sentence of forfeiture of thing by conduct violating the prohibition pursuant to § 70 para. 5, or frustrates confiscation of thing by conduct violating the prohibition pursuant to § 104 para. 2, the court may impose on him confiscation of substitute value up to an amount corresponding to the value of such thing. The court may establish the value of a thing whose confiscation could be imposed on the basis of an expert opinion or expert report. Confiscation of part of assets

(1) The court may impose confiscation of part of assets on an offender who has been found guilty of an intentional criminal offence for which the criminal law provides a custodial sentence with an upper limit of the penalty bracket of at least four years, or the criminal offence of production and other handling of child pornography (§ 192), unauthorised access to a computer system and unauthorised interference with a computer system or information carrier (§ 230), obtaining and possession of an access device and password to a computer system and other such data (§ 231), machinations in public procurement award and in public tender (§ 257), machinations in public auction (§ 258), unauthorised cultivation of plants containing a narcotic or psychotropic substance pursuant to § 285 para.2 to 4, spreading drug addiction (§ 287), bribery (§ 332) or indirect bribery (§ 333), if the offender obtained or attempted to obtain a financial benefit for himself or another by such an offence and the court is of the opinion that a certain part of his property comes from criminal activity having regard to the fact that the value of the property which the offender acquired or transferred to another person or to property in a trust fund during a period of not more than 5 years before committing such an offence, during its commission or after its commission, is grossly disproportionate to the offender's income acquired in accordance with the law or other facts justifying such a conclusion have been established. (2) The court may impose confiscation of part of property in relation to a thing which could otherwise have been confiscated under paragraph 1 if the offender

a) transferred such thing to another person gratuitously or under conspicuously advantageous conditions and that person knew or could and should have known that he transferred such thing to him for the reason of avoiding its confiscation, or that such thing was acquired in contravention of the law,

c) transferred it to a legal person in which he himself or together with close persons has a majority property interest, a majority share of voting rights or decisive influence on management, and the offender uses such thing gratuitously or under conspicuously advantageous conditions,

d) transferred it to property in a trust fund, or

e) acquired it into the community property of spouses. (3) When determining the part of property whose confiscation it imposes, the court shall specify the specific things subject to confiscation. If a gross disproportion between the value of property and the offender's income acquired in accordance with the law during the monitored period has been established, it may specify any things of the offender in value up to the amount of the established gross disproportion. (4) If it is not known where the things that could be confiscated are located, or their confiscation is not appropriate particularly having regard to the rights of third parties, or if the person from whom the thing could have been confiscated destroys, damages, alienates it or otherwise frustrates its confiscation before the decision on confiscation, the court may impose confiscation of substitute value, including a sum of money, up to an amount corresponding to the value of such thing. The value of the thing whose confiscation the court could have imposed shall be determined by the court on the basis of an expert statement or expert opinion. (5) Confiscation also affects the fruits and revenues of a thing which belong to the person from whom such thing is confiscated. Confiscation does not extend to things which are necessary to satisfy the living needs of the person from whom part of property is confiscated, or of persons for whose maintenance or upbringing this person is obliged to provide under the law. Confiscation of writings and devices

(1) A writing or writings with such content whose intentional dissemination with knowledge of their content would constitute the elements of an offence under the criminal law shall be confiscated pursuant to § 101(1) if at least one copy of the writing has already been disseminated, destined for dissemination or prepared. At the same time, a device used or destined for the production of such writings, in particular a printer, plate, mold, typesetting, block, negative, matrix, computer program or copying device, shall also be confiscated; the provisions of § 101(4) and (5) shall apply mutatis mutandis. (2) Confiscation extends only to the copy or copies of writing which are in the possession of persons cooperating in their dissemination or in their preparation for dissemination, or which have been published by display, posting, showing or other similar manner, or which in mail-order dissemination have not yet been delivered to the recipient. (3) Confiscation of a writing or writings pursuant to paragraphs 1 and 2 shall apply mutatis mutandis also in cases where their intentional dissemination with knowledge of their content could constitute an offence if further circumstances occur which, however, were not fulfilled in the case under consideration, and confiscation of the writing or writings is at the same time necessary to prevent their unlawful dissemination. Effect of confiscation

(1) A confiscated thing, confiscated part of property, confiscated substitute value, confiscated writing or device shall become the property of the state; liens on them shall not cease. (2) The provision of § 70(5) shall apply mutatis mutandis to the imposition of confiscation of a thing, confiscation of part of property and to the imposition of an obligation under § 101(4); the prohibition on alienation shall apply here until the fulfilment of the obligation under § 101(4), and if this obligation has not been fulfilled, until the final decision on confiscation of the thing (§ 101(5)). EXPUNGEMENT OF CONVICTION

Conditions for expungement of conviction

(1) The court shall expunge a conviction if the convicted person has led a proper life continuously for a period of at least

a) fifteen years, if it concerns a conviction to an exceptional sentence,

b) ten years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding five years,

c) five years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding one year,

d) three years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding one year or to a sentence of expulsion,

e) one year, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of house arrest, to a sentence of forfeiture of property, to a sentence of confiscation of a thing, to a sentence of prohibition of residence, to a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events or to a pecuniary penalty for a particularly serious felony. (2) If it concerns a conviction to loss of honorary titles or decorations or to loss of military rank, the period specified in paragraph 1 shall be governed by the length of the sentence of imprisonment alongside which the specified sentence was imposed. (3) If the convicted person has demonstrated by his very good conduct after the execution or remission of the sentence or prescription of its execution that he has reformed, the court may, having regard to the interests protected by the criminal law, expunge the conviction at the request of the convicted person or of a person entitled to offer a guarantee for the completion of the convicted person's reform, even before the expiry of the period specified in paragraph 1. (4) The period specified in paragraph 1, in the case where a conditionally released person is deemed to have served the sentence on the day he was conditionally released, shall be governed by the length of actual service of the sentence; in the case where the sentence was reduced by a decision of the President of the Czech Republic, it shall be governed by the length of the sentence thus reduced. (5) If several sentences have been imposed on the offender concurrently, the conviction cannot be expunged unless the period for expungement of the sentence for which this Act prescribes the longest period has elapsed. (6) The provision of paragraph 5 shall apply mutatis mutandis also to the case where several sentences have been imposed on the offender concurrently for which, according to this Act, the effect may occur that the offender is deemed not to have been convicted. (7) Even in the case where the period specified in paragraph 1 has elapsed, the conviction cannot be expunged if an imposed protective measure has not yet been executed or otherwise concluded; this provision shall apply mutatis mutandis also where the criminal law provides that the offender is deemed not to have been convicted. Effects of expungement

If a conviction has been expunged, the offender shall be deemed not to have been convicted. SPECIAL PROVISIONS ON CERTAIN OFFENDERS

Offender of an offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group

(1) An offender of an offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group (§ 129) is a person who has committed an intentional offence as a member of an organised criminal group, or a person who has committed such an act knowingly with a member of an organised criminal group or with the intention of assisting an organised criminal group. (2) The fact that the offender committed the offence as a member of an organised group or in connection with an organised group does not preclude him from being concurrently punished as an offender of an offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group if the conditions prescribed by this Act are met. Imposition of a sentence of imprisonment on an offender of an offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group

(1) The upper limit of the sentencing range for a sentence of imprisonment prescribed in the criminal law shall be increased by one third for an offender of an offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group. The court shall impose on such an offender a sentence of imprisonment in the upper half of the sentencing range for imprisonment thus prescribed, unless the conditions for an extraordinary reduction of the sentence of imprisonment under § 58 are concurrently met. (2) The upper limit of the sentencing range for a sentence of imprisonment may, after the increase under paragraph 1, exceed twenty years. When imposing an exceptional sentence of imprisonment of more than twenty up to thirty years, the upper limit must not exceed thirty years. Juveniles

The criminal liability of juveniles and the sanctions imposed on them are governed by the Act on Juvenile Justice. Unless the Act on Juvenile Justice provides otherwise, this Act shall apply. INTERPRETATIVE PROVISIONS

Criminal law

By criminal law is meant this Act and according to the nature of the matter also the Act on Juvenile Justice and the Act on Criminal Liability of Legal Persons and Proceedings Against Them. On the concept of an offence

By an offence is meant only a judicially punishable act, and unless something else follows from an individual provision of the criminal law, also preparation for an offence, attempt of an offence, masterminding, instigation and aiding. Omission

By an act is also meant an omission of such conduct to which the offender was obliged under another legal regulation, official decision or contract, as a result of voluntary assumption of the obligation to act or if such his special obligation followed from his previous endangering conduct or to which he was for other reasons according to the circumstances and his situation obliged. On the concept of offender

By offender is meant, unless something else follows from an individual provision of the criminal law, also a co-offender and a participant. Specific and special subject

(1) If the criminal law requires a special attribute, capacity or position of the offender for the commission of an offence, only a person who has the required attribute, capacity or position can be the offender or co-offender of the offence. Only a soldier can be the offender or co-offender of a military offence under Chapter Twelve of the Special Part of this Act. (2) If the law provides that the offender must be a bearer of a special attribute, capacity or position, it suffices that the special attribute, capacity or position are present in the legal person on whose behalf the offender acts. This provision shall also apply if

a) the offender's act took place before the creation of the legal person,

b) the legal person was created but the court decided on the invalidity of the legal person, or

c) the legal act which was to establish the authorisation to act for the legal person is invalid or ineffective. (3) The mastermind, instigator or aider of an offence referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2 may also be a person who does not have the attribute, capacity or position required there. (4) Where this Act speaks of a soldier, it means

b) a soldier outside active service if he is in service uniform,

c) a member of a security corps for offences of disobedience to an order (§ 375), disobedience to an order by negligence (§ 376), insult between soldiers (§ 378), insult between soldiers by violence or threat of violence (§ 379), insult to a soldier of equal rank by violence or threat of violence (§ 380), violence against a superior (§ 381), breach of duty of guard service (§ 389), breach of duty of supervisory or other service (§ 390) and establishment, support and propagation of a movement aimed at suppressing human rights and freedoms under § 403(2)(c), or

(5) Where this Act speaks of military service or military duty, it means service or duty of the persons specified in paragraph 4. Unauthorised absence

a) does not commence service at the appointed time,

b) absents himself from the performance of service without permission,

c) is separated in a combat situation from the place of performance of service and after the cessation of the cause of separation does not report to any superior or other military unit, even in the case of return from captivity or after release from captivity. Continuing offence

By a continuing offence is meant such conduct whose individual partial attacks led by a unified intent fulfil, even in aggregate, the elements of the same offence, are connected by the same or similar manner of execution and by close temporal connection and connection in the subject of the attack. Public commission of an offence

An offence is committed publicly if it is committed

a) by the content of a printed matter or disseminated writing, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective manner, or

b) before at least three persons simultaneously present. Commission of an offence with a weapon

An offence is committed with a weapon if the offender or with his knowledge one of the co-offenders uses a weapon for attack, to overcome or prevent resistance or if he has it on him for that purpose; by weapon is here meant, unless something else follows from an individual provision of the criminal law, anything by which an attack on the body can be made more forceful. Commission of an offence by violence

An offence is committed by violence also if it is committed on a person whom the offender rendered defenceless by deceit or other similar manner. Inducing someone into error and exploiting someone's error through technical equipment

Inducing someone into error or exploiting someone's error can also be done by making an intervention into data stored in a computer system or on an information carrier, an intervention into the program or technical equipment of a computer system or by performing another operation in a computer system, an intervention into electronic or other technical equipment, including an intervention into objects serving to control such equipment, or by exploiting such an operation or such an intervention performed by another. Breaking in

By breaking in is meant entering a closed space by deceit, unlawful overcoming of locking or overcoming of another securing obstacle using force. Bodily harm and serious bodily harm

(1) By bodily harm is meant such a state consisting in impairment of health or other illness which, by disruption of normal bodily or mental functions, makes more difficult, not merely for a short time, the injured person's usual way of life and which requires medical treatment. (2) By serious bodily harm is meant only serious impairment of health or other serious illness. Under these conditions, serious bodily harm is

b) loss or substantial reduction of working capacity,
