(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years, if

a) he organizes the act referred to in paragraph 1,

b) he commits such act with a weapon,

c) he commits such act with the intention of concealing or facilitating another criminal offense,

d) he causes serious bodily harm by such act,

e) he causes substantial damage by such act, or

f) he commits such act during a state of emergency or during a state of war. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years, if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1

b) serious bodily harm to at least two persons, or

False Accusation

(1) Whoever falsely accuses another of a criminal offense shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever falsely accuses another of a criminal offense with the intention of causing his criminal prosecution shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 with the intention of obtaining benefit for himself or another,

b) if he commits such act by press, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means,

c) if he commits such act with the intention of seriously harming another in employment, disrupting his family relations or causing him other serious harm,

d) if he commits such act with the intention of concealing or mitigating his own criminal offense, or

e) if he commits such act against another who was performing his duty towards him arising from his employment, occupation, position or office or imposed on him by law. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organized group,

b) if he causes substantial damage by such act, or

c) if he obtains substantial benefit for himself or another by such act. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years,

a) if he causes damage on a large scale by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or

b) if he obtains benefit on a large scale for himself or another by such act. False Testimony and False Expert Opinion

(1) Whoever as an expert submits a false, grossly distorted or incomplete expert opinion shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever as a witness or expert before a court or before an international judicial body, before a notary as a judicial commissioner, a public prosecutor or before a police authority conducting preliminary proceedings under the Criminal Procedure Code, or before an investigative commission of the Chamber of Deputies of the Parliament of the Czech Republic

a) states an untruth about a circumstance that is of substantial significance for a decision or for the findings of the investigative commission of the Chamber of Deputies of the Parliament of the Czech Republic, or shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or by prohibition of activity. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 with the intention of obtaining benefit for himself or another, or

b) if he commits such act with the intention of seriously harming another in employment, disrupting his family relations or causing him other serious harm. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organized group,

b) if he causes substantial damage by such act, or

c) if he obtains substantial benefit for himself or another by such act. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years,

a) if he causes damage on a large scale by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or

b) if he obtains benefit on a large scale for himself or another by such act. False Interpretation

(1) Whoever as an interpreter incorrectly, grossly distortedly or incompletely interprets or translates in writing in proceedings before a public authority or in connection with such proceedings facts or circumstances that are of substantial significance for the decision of the public authority shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever as an interpreter before a court or before an international judicial body, before a notary as a judicial commissioner, a public prosecutor or before a police authority conducting preliminary proceedings under the Criminal Procedure Code, or before an investigative commission of the Chamber of Deputies of the Parliament of the Czech Republic incorrectly, grossly distortedly or incompletely interprets or translates in writing shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or by prohibition of activity. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 with the intention of obtaining benefit for himself or another, or

b) if he commits such act with the intention of seriously harming another in employment, disrupting his family relations or causing him other serious harm. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organized group,

b) if he causes substantial damage by such act, or

c) if he obtains substantial benefit for himself or another by such act. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years,

a) if he causes damage on a large scale by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or

b) if he obtains benefit on a large scale for himself or another by such act. Obstructing Justice

(1) Whoever submits for the purposes of initiating proceedings before a court, before an international judicial body or criminal proceedings or in such proceedings a material or documentary means of evidence that is of substantial significance for a decision, which he knows to be forged or altered, with the intention that it be used as genuine, or forges or alters such means of evidence with the intention that it be used as genuine, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever himself or through another provides, offers or promises a benefit to another or for another for the purpose of committing the criminal offense of false accusation (§ 345), false testimony and false expert opinion (§ 346) or false interpretation (§ 347) shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 with the intention of obtaining benefit for himself or another,

b) if he commits such act as an official person,

c) if he commits such act with the intention of seriously harming another in employment, disrupting his family relations or causing him other serious harm, or

d) if he commits such act against another who was performing his duty towards him arising from his employment, occupation, position or office or imposed on him by law. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organized group,

b) if he causes substantial damage by such act, or

c) if he obtains substantial benefit for himself or another by such act. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years,

a) if he causes damage on a large scale by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or

b) if he obtains benefit on a large scale for himself or another by such act. Forgery and Alteration of Public Document

(1) Whoever forges a public document or substantially alters its content with the intention that it be used as genuine, or uses such document as genuine, whoever obtains such document for himself or another or keeps it with the intention that it be used as genuine, or whoever manufactures, offers, sells, arranges or otherwise makes available, obtains for himself or another or keeps an instrument, device or its component, procedure, aid or any other means, including computer program, created or adapted for forging or altering a public document, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or by prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organized group,

b) if he causes substantial damage by such act, or

c) if he obtains substantial benefit for himself or another by such act. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organized group operating in more than one state,

b) if he commits such act with the intention of enabling or facilitating the commission of a terrorist criminal offense, a criminal offense of participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism under § 312e paragraph 3 or threatening with a terrorist criminal offense (§ 312f),

c) if he causes damage on a large scale by such act, or

d) if he obtains benefit on a large scale for himself or another by such act. Unauthorized Production and Possession of Seal of State Seal and Official Stamp

Whoever unlawfully produces, obtains for himself or another or keeps a seal of a state seal or a stamp of a public authority bearing the image of the state emblem or a stamp whose impression is a mandatory component of public documents, or an object capable of performing their function, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year or by prohibition of activity. Forgery and Issuance of False Medical Report, Opinion and Finding

(1) Whoever forges a medical report, opinion or finding or substantially alters their content with the intention of using them in proceedings before a social security authority or before another public authority, in criminal, civil or other judicial proceedings, or whoever uses such reports, opinions or findings as genuine in proceedings before a social security authority or before another public authority, in criminal, civil or other judicial proceedings, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity. whoever as a physician or other qualified health professional issues a false or grossly distorted medical report, opinion or finding or conceals essential facts about his own or another's health condition therein, so that it may be used in proceedings before a social security authority or before another public authority, in criminal, civil or other judicial proceedings, or whoever uses such medical reports, opinions or findings in proceedings before a social security authority or before another public authority, in criminal, civil or other judicial proceedings. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years or by a pecuniary punishment,

a) if he obtains substantial benefit for himself or another by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or

b) if he causes substantial damage by such act. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years,

a) if he obtains benefit on a large scale for himself or another by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or

b) if he causes damage on a large scale by such act. Obstructing Preparation and Course of Elections and Referendum

Whoever prevents another by violence or threat of violence or by deceit from exercising the right to vote or the right to vote in a referendum or forces another in such manner to exercise the right to vote or the right to vote in a referendum, whoever provides, offers or promises to another or for another in connection with the exercise of the right to vote or the right to vote in a referendum a financial, property or other similar benefit in order that he vote or vote in contravention of the independent expression of his will, whoever forges data in a document on the number of members of a political party or on a petition for electoral purposes or in another document related to elections or a referendum or knowingly uses such document as genuine, whoever knowingly incorrectly counts votes or violates the secrecy of voting, or whoever otherwise in a gross manner obstructs the preparation or course of elections to a legislative body or to a representative body of a territorial self-governing unit or the preparation or course of a referendum, until the announcement of their results, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. Criminal Offenses Disrupting the Coexistence of People

Violence against a Group of Inhabitants and against an Individual

(1) Whoever threatens a group of inhabitants with murder, bodily harm or causing damage on a large scale shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) Whoever uses violence against a group of inhabitants or an individual or threatens them with murder, bodily harm or causing damage on a large scale because of their actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because they are actually or supposedly without religion shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. (3) The same punishment as in paragraph 2 shall be imposed on,

a) whoever conspires or assembles to commit such act, or

b) whoever commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 by press, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means. Dangerous Threatening

(1) Whoever threatens another with murder, serious bodily harm or other serious harm in such a manner that it may arouse justified fear shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year or by prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or by prohibition of activity, if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) as a member of an organized group,

b) against a child or a pregnant woman,

d) against a witness, expert or interpreter in connection with the performance of their duty, or

e) against a health care worker in the performance of health care employment or occupation aimed at saving life or protecting health or against another who was performing his similar duty in the protection of life, health or property arising from his employment, occupation, position or office or imposed on him by law. Dangerous Stalking

(1) Whoever persistently stalks another by

a) threatening to cause bodily harm or other harm to him or to persons close to him,

b) seeking his personal proximity or following him,

c) persistently contacting him through means of electronic communications, in writing or otherwise,

d) restricting him in his usual way of life, or

e) misusing his personal data for the purpose of obtaining personal or other contact, and this conduct is capable of arousing in him justified fear for his life or health or for the life and health of persons close to him, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year or by prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years, if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) against a child or a pregnant woman, Defamation of a Nation, Race, Ethnic or Other Group of Persons

a) a nation, its language, a race or ethnic group, or

b) a group of persons because of their actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because they are actually or supposedly without religion, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) with at least two persons, or

b) by press, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means. Incitement to Hatred against a Group of Persons or to Restriction of Their Rights and Freedoms

(1) Whoever publicly incites hatred against a nation, race, ethnic group, religion, class or other group of persons or to restriction of the rights and freedoms of their members shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever conspires or assembles to commit the act referred to in paragraph 1. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 by press, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means, or

b) if he actively participates by such act in the activities of a group, organization or association that professes discrimination, violence or racial, ethnic, class, religious or other hatred. Other Disturbances of Public Order

Spreading Alarming News

(1) Whoever intentionally causes the danger of serious disquiet of at least part of the population of a place by spreading alarming news that is untrue shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever reports the news referred to in paragraph 1 or other untrue news that is capable of causing measures leading to the danger of serious disquiet of at least part of the population of a place or unjustified rescue work of the integrated rescue system to a court, an authority of the Police of the Czech Republic, an authority of state administration, territorial self-government, or another public authority, a legal person, a natural person who is an entrepreneur, or to a mass media, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years or by prohibition of activity. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 repeatedly,

b) if he commits such act as a member of an organized group,

c) if he causes substantial damage by such act,

d) if he causes serious disruption to the activities of an authority of state administration, territorial self-government, a court or another public authority by such act, or

e) if he causes serious disruption to the activities of a legal or natural person who is an entrepreneur by such act. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years,

a) if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 during a state of emergency or during a state of war, during a natural disaster or other event seriously endangering the life or health of people, public order or property, or

b) if he causes damage on a large scale by such act. Hooliganism

(1) Whoever commits publicly or in a place accessible to the public gross indecency or hooliganism, in particular by assaulting another, defaming a grave, historical or cultural monument, or grossly disrupts the preparation, course or conclusion of an organized sporting event, assembly or ceremony of people, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

b) as a member of an organized group. Defamation of Human Remains

(1) Whoever unlawfully opens a grave or tomb or an urn with human remains shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever arbitrarily removes human remains from a burial site or handles human remains in violation of the law. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) as a member of an organized group,

b) with the intention of obtaining property benefit for himself or another, or

c) with the intention of concealing or facilitating another criminal offense. Intoxication

(1) Whoever brings himself, even negligently, by ingestion or application of an addictive substance into a state of insanity in which he commits an otherwise punishable act shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years; however, if he commits an otherwise punishable act for which the law provides a milder punishment, he shall be punished by that milder punishment. (2) The provision of paragraph 1, as well as § 26 shall not apply if the offender brought himself into a state of insanity with the intention of committing a criminal offense, or committed a criminal offense through negligence consisting in bringing himself into a state of insanity. Organized Criminal Group

Participation in an Organized Criminal Group

(1) Whoever establishes an organized criminal group, whoever participates in the activities of an organized criminal group, or whoever supports an organized criminal group shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years or by forfeiture of property. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to twelve years or by forfeiture of property, if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a leading member or representative of an organized criminal group. (3) The provisions of §§ 107 and 108 shall not apply to the offender of the act referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2. Special Provision on Effective Remorse

Whoever commits the act referred to in § 361 is not punishable if he reported the organized criminal group to a public prosecutor or a police authority at a time when the danger that arose to the interest protected by this law from another act of the organized criminal group than that referred to in § 361 could still be eliminated. A soldier may make such report also to a superior. Impunity of Agent

(1) An agent who participates in the activities of an organized criminal group or supports an organized criminal group is not punishable for the criminal offense of participation in an organized criminal group under § 361 if he committed such act with the aim of detecting the offender of criminal activity committed for the benefit of the organized criminal group or preventing its commission. (2) An agent who participates in the activities of an organized criminal group or as a member of an organized group or supports such a group is not punishable for the criminal offenses referred to in § 312c paragraph 2 if he committed such act with the aim of detecting the offender of criminal activity committed by members of the organized group, in connection with the organized group or committed for the benefit of the organized criminal group or preventing its commission. (3) An agent who established or organized an organized criminal group or an organized group or an agent who is a leading member or representative of an organized criminal group does not become unpunishable. Certain Other Forms of Criminal Complicity

Incitement to a Criminal Offense

Whoever publicly incites to a criminal offense shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. Approval of a Criminal Offense

(1) Whoever publicly approves a committed felony or whoever publicly praises the offender for a felony shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever with the intention of expressing agreement with a criminal offense

a) rewards or compensates the offender or a person close to him for the punishment, or

b) organizes a collection for such reward or compensation. Harboring

(1) Whoever assists the offender of a criminal offense with the intention of enabling him to escape criminal prosecution, punishment or protective measure or their execution shall be punished by imprisonment for up to four years; however, if he thus assists the offender of a criminal offense for which the criminal law provides a milder punishment, he shall be punished by that milder punishment. (2) Whoever commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 for the benefit of a person close to him is not punishable, unless he did so with the intention of

a) assisting a person who committed the criminal offense of high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), threatening with a terrorist criminal offense (§ 312f), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), aggression (§ 405a), preparation of an aggressive war (§ 406), use of prohibited combat means and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of the population (§ 413), pillage in the area of war operations (§ 414) or misuse of internationally recognized and state symbols (§ 415), or

b) obtaining property benefit for himself or another. Failure to Prevent a Criminal Offense

(1) Whoever learns in a credible manner that another is preparing or committing the criminal offense of murder (§ 140), manslaughter (§ 141), serious bodily harm (§ 145), torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment (§ 149), unlawful termination of pregnancy without the consent of the pregnant woman (§ 159), unauthorized removal of tissues and organs (§ 164), trafficking in human beings (§ 168), deprivation of personal freedom (§ 170), abduction under § 172 paragraphs 2 and 3, robbery (§ 173), hostage taking (§ 174), extortion under § 175 paragraphs 3 and 4, unauthorized handling of personal data under § 180 paragraph 4, rape (§ 185), sexual abuse (§ 187), abuse of a child for the production of pornography (§ 193), abuse of a person entrusted to one's care (§ 198), theft under § 205 paragraph 5, embezzlement under § 206 paragraph 5, fraud under § 209 paragraph 5, insurance fraud under § 210 paragraph 6, credit fraud under § 211 paragraph 6, subsidy fraud under § 212 paragraph 6, legalization of proceeds of crime under § 216 paragraphs 4 and 5, forgery and alteration of money (§ 233), unauthorized procurement, forgery and alteration of a payment instrument (§ 234), unauthorized production of money (§ 237), misuse of information in business dealings under § 255 paragraph 4, abuse of position in business dealings under § 255a paragraph 3, damaging the financial interests of the European Union under § 260 paragraph 5, violation of regulations on export control of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 262), breach of obligations in the export of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 263), conducting foreign trade in military material without a permit or license (§ 265), breach of obligation in connection with the issuance of a permit and license for foreign trade in military material (§ 266), general endangerment (§ 272), development, production and possession of prohibited combat means (§ 280), unauthorized production and possession of radioactive material and highly dangerous substance (§ 281), unauthorized production and possession of nuclear material and special fissile material (§ 282), unauthorized production and other handling of narcotic and psychotropic substances and poisons (§ 283), seizure of control of an aircraft, civilian vessel and fixed platform (§ 290), abduction of an aircraft abroad (§ 292), high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), threatening with a terrorist criminal offense (§ 312f), sabotage (§ 314), espionage (§ 316), endangering classified information (§ 317), war treason (§ 320), violence against a public authority under § 323 paragraphs 3 and 4, violence against an official person under § 325 paragraphs 3 and 4, acceptance of a bribe (§ 331), bribery (§ 332), violent crossing of the state border under § 339 paragraphs 2 and 3, organizing and enabling unlawful crossing of the state border under § 340 paragraph 4, mutiny of prisoners (§ 344), disobedience to an order under § 375 paragraphs 2 and 3, inciting and coercing to breach military duty under § 377 paragraphs 2 and 3, violation of rights and protected interests of soldiers of equal rank under § 382 paragraphs 3 and 4, violation of rights and protected interests of subordinate soldiers or soldiers of lower rank under § 383 paragraphs 3 and 4, desertion (§ 386), endangering the moral state of soldiers under § 392 paragraph 2, genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), aggression (§ 405a), preparation of an aggressive war (§ 406), contacts endangering peace (§ 409), use of prohibited combat means and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of the population (§ 413), pillage in the area of war operations (§ 414) or misuse of internationally recognized and state symbols under § 415 paragraph 3, and does not prevent the commission or completion of such criminal offense, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years; however, if this law provides a milder punishment for any of these criminal offenses, he shall be punished by that milder punishment. (2) Whoever commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 is not punishable if he could not prevent the criminal offense without considerable difficulty or without putting himself or a person close to him in danger of death, bodily harm, other serious harm or criminal prosecution.However, placing a close person in danger of criminal prosecution does not exempt the perpetrator from criminal liability if the failure to prevent concerns the criminal offense of high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), threatening with a terrorist criminal offense (§ 312f), sabotage (§ 314), espionage (§ 316), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), aggression (§ 405a), preparation of an aggressive war (§ 406), use of a prohibited means of warfare and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of the population (§ 413), pillaging in the area of military operations (§ 414), and abuse of internationally recognized and state symbols according to § 415 paragraph 3. (3) A criminal offense may be prevented also by its timely notification to a state attorney or a police authority; a soldier may instead make the notification to a superior. Failure to Report a Criminal Offense

(1) Whoever learns in a credible manner that another has committed the criminal offense of murder (§ 140), grievous bodily harm (§ 145), torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment (§ 149), trafficking in human beings (§ 168), deprivation of personal liberty (§ 170), hostage-taking (§ 174), child abuse for the production of pornography (§ 193), abuse of a person entrusted to one's care (§ 198), counterfeiting and alteration of currency (§ 233), unauthorized procurement, counterfeiting and alteration of a means of payment (§ 234), unauthorized production of currency (§ 237), breach of regulations on export control of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 262), breach of obligations in the export of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 263), conduct of foreign trade in military material without authorization or license (§ 265), breach of obligation in connection with issuing authorization and license for foreign trade in military material (§ 266), general endangerment (§ 272), development, production and possession of prohibited means of warfare (§ 280), unauthorized production and possession of radioactive substance and highly dangerous substance (§ 281), unauthorized production and possession of nuclear material and special fissile material (§ 282), seizure of control of an aircraft, civil vessel and fixed platform (§ 290), hijacking of an aircraft to a foreign country (§ 292), high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), sabotage (§ 314), espionage (§ 316), endangering classified information (§ 317), war treason (§ 320), acceptance of a bribe (§ 331), bribery (§ 332), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), aggression (§ 405a), preparation of an aggressive war (§ 406), use of a prohibited means of warfare and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of the population (§ 413), pillaging in the area of military operations (§ 414), or abuse of internationally recognized and state symbols according to § 415 paragraph 3, and fails to report such criminal offense without delay to a state attorney or a police authority, or instead of that, if he is a soldier, to a superior, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years; if this Act provides for a lighter sentence for any of these criminal offenses, he shall be punished by that lighter sentence. (2) Whoever commits the act specified in paragraph 1 is not criminally liable if he could not make the notification without placing himself or a close person in danger of death, injury to health, other serious harm or criminal prosecution. (3) The notification obligation under paragraph 1 does not apply to an attorney or his employee who learns of the commission of a criminal offense in connection with the practice of law or legal practice. The notification obligation also does not apply to a clergyman of a registered church and religious society with authorization to exercise special rights, if he learns of the commission of a criminal offense in connection with the exercise of the seal of confession or in connection with the exercise of a right similar to the seal of confession. The notification obligation for the criminal offense of trafficking in human beings according to § 168 paragraph 2 and deprivation of personal liberty (§ 170) also does not apply to a person providing assistance to victims of criminal offenses. CRIMINAL OFFENSES AGAINST MILITARY SERVICE OBLIGATION

Rendering Unfit for Service

(1) Whoever renders himself or another permanently or temporarily unfit or less fit to fulfill military service obligation in peacetime shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for one to ten years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Failure to Fulfill Conscription Obligation

(1) Whoever fails to appear for conscription proceedings with the intent not to fulfill his conscription obligation or to delay its fulfillment shall be punished by imprisonment for up to six months. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Evasion of Military Service Obligation

(1) Whoever engages in machinations with the intent to obtain relief from fulfilling military service obligation shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) Whoever engages in machinations with the intent to

a) himself evade fulfilling military service obligation wholly or in part, or

b) exempt another wholly or in part from fulfilling military service obligation, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to two years. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Failure to Report for Service in the Armed Forces

Whoever, on the basis of a call-up order duly delivered to him, fails to report during a state of emergency for service in the armed forces within 24 hours after expiration of the time limit specified in such call-up order, although he was not prevented by any obstacle envisaged by law, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. Failure to Report for Extraordinary Service in the Armed Forces

(1) Whoever, on the basis of a call-up order duly delivered to him, fails to report during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war for extraordinary service in the armed forces within 24 hours after expiration of the time limit specified in such call-up order, although he was not prevented by any obstacle envisaged by law, shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. (2) Whoever, in the event that he does not receive a call-up order, fails to report during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war for extraordinary service in the armed forces on the basis of a public proclamation or mobilization call, although he was not prevented by any obstacle envisaged by law, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. Failure to Report for Extraordinary Service in the Armed Forces through Negligence

(1) Whoever, on the basis of a call-up order duly delivered to him, negligently fails to report during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war for extraordinary service in the armed forces within 24 hours after expiration of the time limit specified in such call-up order, although he was not prevented by any obstacle envisaged by law, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) Whoever, in the event that he does not receive a call-up order, negligently fails to report during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war for extraordinary service in the armed forces on the basis of a public proclamation or mobilization call, although he was not prevented by any obstacle envisaged by law, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. MILITARY CRIMINAL OFFENSES

Criminal Offenses Against Military Subordination and Military Honor

Disobedience of an Order

(1) Whoever refuses to execute or intentionally fails to execute an order shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 with a group of soldiers,

b) he commits such act with a weapon,

c) he commits such act under circumstances where he could thereby frustrate or substantially impede the fulfillment of an important service task,

d) he causes by such act endangerment of the combat readiness of a military unit, military facility, military rescue unit or other organizational unit within the competence of the Ministry of Defense,

e) he causes by such act damage on a large scale or damage to the environment requiring costs at least on a large scale for its removal,

f) he causes by such act grievous bodily harm, or

g) he causes death by such act. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. Disobedience of an Order through Negligence

(1) Whoever negligently fails to execute an order and thereby frustrates or substantially impedes the fulfillment of an important service task shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 he causes

a) serious endangerment of the combat readiness of a military unit, military facility, military rescue unit or other organizational unit within the competence of the Ministry of Defense,

b) damage on a large scale or damage to the environment requiring costs at least on a large scale for its removal,

c) grievous bodily harm, or

d) death. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. Resistance and Coercion to Breach Military Duty

(1) Whoever resists a soldier who is fulfilling a military duty or coerces him to breach a military duty shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 with a group of persons,

b) he commits such act with a weapon,

c) he commits such act during a combat situation, or

d) he causes grievous bodily harm by such act. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if

a) he causes death by the act specified in paragraph 1, or

b) he commits the act specified in paragraph 2 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Insult Between Soldiers

(1) A soldier who insults

a) a superior or a soldier with a higher rank,

b) a subordinate or a soldier with a lower rank, or

c) a soldier of the same rank at a time when he himself or the insulted person is performing guard, supervisory or other service, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 against another because of his actual or presumed race, membership in an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion, or because he is actually or allegedly without religion. Insult Between Soldiers by Violence or Threat of Violence

(1) Whoever insults by violence or threat of immediate violence

a) a superior or a soldier with a higher rank, or

b) a subordinate or a soldier with a lower rank, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 at a time when he himself or the insulted person is performing guard, supervisory or other service,

b) he commits such act against a military guard,

c) he commits such act with a weapon or with at least two persons,

d) he commits such act against another because of his actual or presumed race, membership in an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion, or because he is actually or allegedly without religion, or

e) he causes injury to health by such act. Insult of a Soldier of the Same Rank by Violence or Threat of Violence

(1) Whoever insults by violence or threat of immediate violence a soldier of the same rank at a time when he himself or the insulted person is performing guard, supervisory or other service shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 against a military guard,

b) he commits such act with a weapon or with at least two persons,

c) he commits such act against another because of his actual or presumed race, membership in an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion, or because he is actually or allegedly without religion, or

d) he causes injury to health by such act. Violence Against a Superior

(1) Whoever uses violence against a superior

a) with the intent to influence the performance of his military duties, or

b) because of the performance of his military duties, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if

a) he causes grievous bodily harm by the act specified in paragraph 1, or

b) he commits such act with a weapon or with at least two persons. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if

a) he causes death by the act specified in paragraph 1, or

b) he commits such act during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. Violation of Rights and Protected Interests of Soldiers of the Same Rank

(1) Whoever compels a soldier of the same rank to perform personal services or restricts him in his rights or arbitrarily impedes the performance of his service shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 by violence or threat of violence or threat of other serious harm,

b) he commits such act with at least two persons,

c) he commits such act against another because of his actual or presumed race, membership in an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion, or because he is actually or allegedly without religion, or

d) he causes injury to health by such act. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 in a particularly brutal manner or with a weapon,

b) he causes grievous bodily harm by such act, or

c) he commits such act during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. (4) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if he causes death by the act specified in paragraph 1. Violation of Rights and Protected Interests of Subordinate Soldiers or Soldiers with Lower Rank

(1) Whoever compels a subordinate soldier or a soldier with a lower rank to perform personal services or restricts him in his rights or arbitrarily impedes the performance of his service, or imposes on him a disciplinary punishment in violation of another legal regulation, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 by violence or threat of violence or threat of other serious harm,

b) he commits such act with at least two persons,

c) he commits such act against another because of his actual or presumed race, membership in an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion, or because he is actually or allegedly without religion, or

d) he causes injury to health by such act. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 in a particularly brutal manner or with a weapon,

b) he causes grievous bodily harm by such act, or

c) he commits such act during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. (4) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if he causes death by the act specified in paragraph 1. Criminal Offenses Against the Duty to Perform Military Service

Evading the Performance of Service

(1) Whoever during a state of emergency injures himself, feigns illness, forges a document, abuses an addictive substance or uses another artifice with the intent to evade the performance of service shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. Evading the Performance of Service through Negligence

Whoever during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation negligently renders himself unfit to perform service by abusing an addictive substance shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. Desertion

(1) Whoever absents himself without authorization with the intent to evade military service shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 with a weapon. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Unauthorized Absence

(1) Whoever absents himself without authorization by failing to report in time for departure or takeoff of military transport shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) Whoever absents himself without authorization for a period longer than three days, whoever absents himself without authorization repeatedly for a period longer than 24 hours although he was already disciplinarily punished for such act in the last year, whoever absents himself without authorization for a period longer than two days during the performance of particularly important tasks, or whoever during service abroad absents himself without authorization for a period longer than 24 hours, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (3) Whoever absents himself without authorization for a period longer than fourteen days, whoever absents himself without authorization for a period longer than six days during the performance of particularly important tasks, or whoever during service abroad absents himself without authorization for a period longer than three days, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years. Criminal Offenses Against Duties of Guard, Supervisory or Other Service

Evading the Performance of Guard, Supervisory or Other Service

(1) Whoever injures himself, feigns illness, forges a document, abuses an addictive substance or uses another artifice with the intent to evade the performance of guard, supervisory or other service shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for one to ten years or an exceptional sentence if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state, during a state of war or during a combat situation. Breach of Guard Service Duty

(1) Whoever in guard service or other similar service breaches, even through negligence, the regulations or rules of this service or special orders issued pursuant thereto shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 in the performance of guard service that has particularly important state or military significance,

b) he commits such act by gross breach of his duty,

c) he causes by such act a harmful consequence which the performance of guard service or other similar service was intended to avert, or

d) he commits such act during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he intentionally commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a combat situation and there is one of the circumstances specified in paragraph 2. Breach of Supervisory or Other Service Duty

(1) Whoever in supervisory or other service, even through negligence, breaches the regulations or rules of this service shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 he causes a particularly serious consequence which he was obligated to prevent. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a combat situation. Breach of Service Duty in Air Defense

(1) Whoever breaches, even through negligence, the rules of service in facilities intended to ensure the security of airspace shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 he causes a particularly serious consequence which he was obligated to prevent. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war and thereby causes a particularly serious consequence which he was obligated to prevent. Criminal Offenses Endangering the Combat Capability of the Armed Forces

Endangering the Morale of Soldiers

(1) Whoever incites soldiers against military service or against a superior, or whoever in a serious manner or systematically undermines military discipline shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war or during a combat situation. Breach of Service Duty by a Soldier

(1) Whoever, to the detriment of the armed forces, by failure to fulfill an imposed service duty according to his service assignment, even through negligence, substantially reduces the usability of military materiel shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever without authorization uses military materiel of greater value for a purpose for which it is not intended, or gives consent to such use, or whoever abuses or enables the abuse of subordinates for tasks beyond the scope of service duties shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if

a) he procures for himself or another a substantial benefit by the act specified in paragraph 2, or

b) he causes reduction of the combat capability of the armed forces or substantial damage by the intentionally committed act specified in paragraph 1 or 2. (4) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if by the intentionally committed act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 he causes damage on a large scale. (5) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if he intentionally commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 during a combat situation, during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Cowardice Before the Enemy

Whoever during a combat situation surrenders into captivity out of cowardice or faintheartedness shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twenty years. Failure to Fulfill a Combat Task

(1) Whoever absents himself without permission from the place of service performance during a combat situation shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twenty years. (2) Whoever during a combat situation evades the duty to fulfill a combat task or refuses to use weapons shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty years. Abandonment of Military Materiel

(1) Whoever during a combat situation discards, abandons or renders unusable military armament or other military equipment shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 he causes reduction of the combat capability of the armed forces or substantial damage. Surrender of Soldiers and Military Materiel to the Enemy

(1) A commander who, without it being necessitated by a combat situation, surrenders soldiers to the enemy or leaves military armament, military equipment or technical facilities to him, even through negligence, shall be punished, if he did not act with the intent to support the enemy, by imprisonment for five to twenty years. Criminal Offenses Against Service Duties of Members of Security Corps

Breach of Service Duty by a Member of a Security Corps

(1) Whoever, to the detriment of a security corps, by failure to fulfill an imposed service duty, even through negligence, substantially reduces the usability of armament, equipment or other material means shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever without authorization uses material means of greater value specified in paragraph 1 for a purpose for which it is not intended, or gives consent to such use, or whoever abuses or enables the abuse of subordinates or inferiors for non-service acts shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if

a) he procures for himself or another a substantial benefit by the act specified in paragraph 2, or

b) he causes substantial damage by the intentionally committed act specified in paragraph 1 or 2. (4) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if by the intentionally committed act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 he causes damage on a large scale. Common Provision

The provisions on the criminal offenses of disobedience of an order (§ 375), disobedience of an order through negligence (§ 376), insult between soldiers (§ 378), insult between soldiers by violence or threat of violence (§ 379), insult of a soldier of the same rank by violence or threat of violence (§ 380), violence against a superior (§ 381), breach of guard service duty (§ 389) and breach of supervisory or other service duty (§ 390) also apply to members of a security corps. CRIMES AGAINST HUMANITY, CRIMES AGAINST PEACE AND WAR CRIMES

Crimes Against Humanity

Genocide

(1) Whoever with the intent to destroy in whole or in part any racial, ethnic, national, religious, class or other similar group of people

a) places members of such group in such living conditions as are intended to bring about their complete or partial physical destruction,

b) implements measures aimed at preventing births within such group,

c) forcibly transfers children from one such group to another, or

d) causes a member of such group grievous bodily harm or death, shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to twenty years or an exceptional sentence. (2) The same punishment shall apply to whoever publicly incites the commission of the act specified in paragraph 1. Attack Against Humanity

(1) Whoever within the framework of a widespread or systematic attack directed against the civilian population commits

a) murder,

b) enslavement,

c) deportation or forcible transfer of a group of population,

d) rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization or any other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity,

e) persecution of a group of population on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural or religious grounds, on the basis of gender or for any other similar reason,

f) apartheid or other similar segregation or discrimination,

g) deprivation of personal liberty, abduction to an unknown place or any other restriction of personal liberty resulting in the enforced disappearance of persons,

h) torture,

i) extermination, or

j) any other inhumane act of a similar nature, shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to twenty years or an exceptional sentence. Apartheid and Discrimination of a Group of People

(1) Whoever practices apartheid or racial, ethnic, national, religious or class segregation or other similar discrimination of a group of people shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if

a) by the act specified in paragraph 1 he subjects such group of people to severe living conditions, or

b) by such act he subjects such group of people to inhuman or degrading treatment. Establishment, Support and Promotion of a Movement Aimed at Suppressing Human Rights and Freedoms

(1) Whoever establishes, supports or promotes a movement that demonstrably aims at suppressing human rights and freedoms, or professes racial, ethnic, national, religious or class hatred or hatred toward another group of persons shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 by print, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means,

b) he commits such act as a member of an organized group,

c) he commits such act as a soldier, or

d) he commits such act during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war. Dissemination of Material for the Promotion of a Movement Aimed at Suppressing Human Rights and Freedoms

(1) Whoever on a larger scale produces, imports, exports, transports through the territory, offers, makes publicly accessible, mediates, puts into circulation, sells or otherwise procures for another or creates for another material that depicts, records or otherwise represents symbols, in particular logos, flags, badges, uniforms and their parts, slogans, sayings, declarations, slogans and forms of greetings, representatives or statements of representatives of the movement specified in § 403 paragraph 1, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, a fine or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or a fine if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) as a member of an organized group,

b) by print, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means, or

c) with the intent to procure a substantial benefit for himself or another. (3) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to six years or a fine if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 with the intent to procure a benefit on a large scale for himself or another. Special Provision on Exemption from Punishment

Pursuant to § 403a, a person is not criminally liable if he produces, imports, exports, transports through the territory, offers, makes publicly accessible, mediates, puts into circulation, sells or otherwise procures for another or creates for another such material for the purpose of education, research, art, reporting on current or historical events or for similar purposes. Expression of Sympathy for a Movement Aimed at Suppressing Human Rights and Freedoms

Whoever publicly expresses sympathy for the movement specified in § 403 paragraph 1 shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. Denial, Questioning, Approval and Justification of Genocide

Whoever publicly denies, questions, approves or attempts to justify Nazi, Communist or other genocide or Nazi, Communist or other crimes against humanity or war crimes or crimes against peace shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. Crimes Against Peace and War Crimes

Aggression

Whoever in a position that enables him to exercise control over a state or to direct its political or military actions, in violation of the provisions of international law, plans, prepares, initiates or carries out an act of aggression, which consists in the use of armed force by such state against the sovereignty, territorial integrity or political independence of another state or in the use of armed force by such state in any other manner inconsistent with the Charter of the United Nations and which by its nature, gravity and scale constitutes a manifest violation of the Charter of the United Nations, shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to twenty years or an exceptional sentence. Preparation of an Aggressive War

Whoever prepares an aggressive war in which the Czech Republic is to participate and thereby brings about for the Czech Republic a danger of war shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to twenty years or an exceptional sentence. Incitement to an Aggressive War

(1) Whoever publicly incites an aggressive war in which the Czech Republic is to participate, propagates such war or otherwise supports war propaganda shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years if

a) he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 at an assembly subject to notification obligation, or

b) he commits such act by print, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means. Common Provision

Preparation and incitement to an aggressive war does not include conduct related to

a) the declaration of a state of war when there is a threat that the Czech Republic will be attacked, or when it is necessary to fulfill international treaty obligations concerning collective defense against attack,

b) the participation of the Czech Republic in the defense systems of an international organization of which the Czech Republic is a member,

c) the sending of armed forces of the Czech Republic outside the territory of the Czech Republic or the stay of armed forces of other states on the territory of the Czech Republic, to which the Parliament of the Czech Republic or the Government of the Czech Republic gives its consent. Contacts Endangering Peace

(1) A citizen of the Czech Republic or a person without citizenship who has been granted permanent residence on its territory, who with the intent to bring about war or armed action against the Czech Republic or another state, himself or through another, establishes or maintains contacts with a foreign power shall be punished by imprisonment for three to twelve years. (2) The perpetrator shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty years or an exceptional sentence if by the act specified in paragraph 1 he causes

a) serious endangerment of the international standing of the Czech Republic or another state, or

b) serious endangerment of the existence of the Czech Republic or another state. Breach of International Sanctions

(1) Whoever on a larger scale breaches a command, prohibition or restriction established for the purpose of maintaining or restoring international peace and security, protection of human rights and freedoms, combating terrorism, observance of international law, support of democracy and the rule of law, compliance with which the Czech Republic is obligated by virtue of its membership in the United Nations or in the European Union or which the Czech Republic has introduced pursuant to the sanctions law, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or a fine.(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if

a) he causes considerable damage by the act referred to in paragraph 1, or

b) he obtains considerable benefit for himself or another by such act.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if

a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 in connection with an organized group operating in multiple states,

b) he commits such act with the intention to obtain benefit of large scale for himself or another,

c) he causes damage of large scale by such act,

d) he causes serious threat to the international position of the Czech Republic by such act, or

e) he substantially contributes by such act to the disruption of international peace and security, measures aimed at the protection of human rights and freedoms, the fight against terrorism, compliance with international law or support of democracy and the rule of law.

Use of prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat

(1) Whoever in war or other armed conflict or in a combat situation

a) orders the use of prohibited means of combat or material of similar nature or uses such means or material, or

b) orders the conduct of combat in a prohibited manner or himself conducts combat in such manner,

shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years.

(2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever, in violation of provisions of international law on means and methods of conducting war or other armed conflict, intentionally

a) harms by military operation the civilian population or civilian persons in life, health or property or conducts an attack against them for reasons of reprisals,

b) conducts an attack against an undefended place or demilitarized zone,

c) destroys or damages a dam, nuclear power plant or similar facility containing dangerous forces, or

d) destroys or damages a facility intended for humanitarian purposes or an internationally recognized cultural or natural monument or misuses such facility or monument for military purposes.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or by an exceptional sentence if he causes by an act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) serious injury to health, or

War cruelty

(1) Whoever in war or other armed conflict violates regulations of international law by treating inhumanely the civilian population, refugees, wounded, sick, members of armed forces who have laid down their arms, or prisoners of war, shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years.

(2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever in war or other armed conflict violates regulations of international law by

a) failing to implement effective measures for the protection of persons who need such assistance, especially children, women, wounded and sick, or prevents such measures, or

b) prevents or hinders civil defense organizations of the enemy, neutral or other state in fulfilling their humanitarian tasks.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or by an exceptional sentence if he causes by an act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) serious injury to health, or

Persecution of the population

(1) Whoever in war or other armed conflict practices apartheid or commits other inhumane acts arising from racial, ethnic, national, religious, class or other similar discrimination or terrorizes the civilian population by violence or threat of its use, shall be punished by imprisonment for five to fifteen years.

(2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever in war or other armed conflict

a) destroys or seriously disrupts sources of basic life necessities of the civilian population in occupied territory or contact zone or arbitrarily fails to provide the population with assistance necessary for survival,

b) unreasonably delays the return of the civilian population or prisoners of war,

c) unreasonably resettles or expels the civilian population of occupied territory,

d) settles occupied territory with the population of his own country,

e) recruits children for service in arms, or

f) arbitrarily prevents the civilian population or prisoners of war from having their offenses decided in impartial judicial proceedings.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty years or by an exceptional sentence if he causes by an act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) serious injury to health, or

Looting in the area of war operations

(1) Whoever in the area of war operations, on the battlefield, in places affected by war operations, armed conflict or in occupied territory

a) robs the fallen or otherwise appropriates another's property, or

b) arbitrarily destroys, damages, removes, conceals or misuses another's property,

shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to twenty years or by an exceptional sentence.

Misuse of internationally recognized and state emblems

(1) Whoever in a state of threat to the state or in a state of war or in war or other armed conflict misuses the emblem of the Red Cross or other distinctive emblems or colors recognized by international law for marking medical institutions or means of transport for medical assistance or evacuation, shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years.

(2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever in war or other armed conflict misuses the emblem of the United Nations or the flag or state or military emblem, insignia or uniform of a neutral or other state that is not a party to the conflict.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twenty years or by an exceptional sentence if he causes by an act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) death or serious injury to health,

b) damage of large scale, or

c) retaliatory measures by the other warring party or the other party to the armed conflict of the same or similar character.

Misuse of flag and truce

(1) Whoever in war or other armed conflict misuses the flag or state or military emblem, insignia or uniform of another state that is a party to the conflict, shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years.

(2) Whoever in war or other armed conflict misuses a declared truce or flag of truce, shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years.

Harm to a parliamentarian

Whoever insults a parliamentarian or member of his entourage, or whoever unlawfully detains such person, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years.

Common provision

Responsibility of a superior

(1) A military or other superior is criminally liable for the criminal offense of genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), aggression (§ 405a), preparation of aggressive war (§ 406), incitement to aggressive war (§ 407), use of prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of the population (§ 413), looting in the area of war operations (§ 414), misuse of internationally recognized and state emblems (§ 415), misuse of flag and truce (§ 416) or harm to a parliamentarian (§ 417) committed by his subordinate, over whom he exercised his authority and control, if, even through negligence, he did not prevent him from committing such criminal offense, did not hinder the commission of such criminal offense or did not punish him for the commission of such criminal offense or did not hand him over to the authority competent for such punishment.

(2) The provisions on criminal liability and punishability of the subordinate offender shall apply to the criminal liability and punishability of a military or other superior.

TRANSITIONAL AND FINAL PROVISIONS

Transitional provision

A sentence imposed before the date of entry into force of this Act for an act that is not a criminal offense under this Act, or its unexecuted remainder, shall not be executed. The provisions on aggregate sentence shall not apply in such case. If an aggregate or cumulative sentence was imposed for such act and a concurrent criminal offense, the court shall proportionally reduce the sentence; in doing so, it shall take into account the mutual ratio of seriousness of acts that are not criminal offenses from the date of entry into force of this Act and concurrent criminal offenses.

Implementation of European Union law

This Act incorporates the relevant regulations of the European Union1).

Effectiveness

This Act enters into force on 1 January 2010.

Transitional provisions introduced by Act No. 357/2011 Coll.

Art. IX

1. The provision of § 11 para. 2 of Act No. 40/2009 Coll., as amended from the date of entry into force of this Act, does not apply to convictions by a court of another Member State of the European Union for a criminal offense committed before the date of entry into force of this Act.

2. The provision of § 11 para. 2 of Act No. 40/2009 Coll., as amended from the date of entry into force of this Act, shall until 27 April 2012 apply only to final convictions by a court of a Member State of the European Union recorded in the register of the Member State of which the convicted person is a citizen or in whose territory he has his habitual residence.

Transitional provision introduced by Act No. 55/2017 Coll.

Art. II

For the purposes of imposing a protective measure of confiscation of part of property according to § 102a of Act No. 40/2009 Coll., as amended from the date of entry into force of this Act, when determining the amount of gross disproportion between the value of property that the offender acquired or transferred to another person or to property in a trust fund or similar institution, and his income acquired in accordance with the law, only property that the offender acquired or transferred to another person or to property in a trust fund or similar institution from the date of entry into force of this Act may be taken into account.

Transitional provisions introduced by Act No. 58/2017 Coll.

Art. II

1. The assignment of convicted persons to serve a sentence of imprisonment in a prison with supervision and in a prison with surveillance shall be changed on the date of entry into force of this Act to assignment to a prison with security.

2. When deciding on the manner of execution of an unconditional sentence of imprisonment imposed for a criminal offense committed before the date of entry into force of this Act, § 56 of the Criminal Code, as amended from the date of entry into force of this Act, shall apply from the date of entry into force of this Act.

3. A proposal for reassignment to another basic type of prison on which a final decision has not been made before the date of entry into force of this Act shall not be taken into account, unless it is a proposal for reassignment from a prison with increased security to a prison with security or a proposal for reassignment from a prison with security to a prison with increased security.

Transitional provision introduced by Act No. 333/2020 Coll.

Art. II

In proceedings commenced before the date of entry into force of this Act, § 138 para. 1 of Act No. 40/2009 Coll., as amended until the date of entry into force of this Act, shall be used for the purposes of determining subject-matter jurisdiction.
